# SAT comparison: thermo-nova (ground truth) vs OTGW firmware

This document compares the SAT (Smart Autotune Thermostat) heating controller in two code bases. The thermo-nova code is the ground truth. The OTGW firmware is a port of it. There is no written SAT design document, so where the two differ, thermo-nova is leading.

## Pinned versions

| Code base | Version | Paths in citations | Source of the pin |
|---|---|---|---|
| thermo-nova (ground truth) | Alexwijn/SAT, branch `releases/thermo-nova`, commit `7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb` (2026-03-18) | relative to `custom_components/sat`, for example `pwm.py:120-134` | Local copy `other-projects/SAT-releases-thermo-nova`. The `other-projects` submodule commit `ce79538c` is titled "Record the upstream commit the thermo-nova snapshot mirrors (7beaaf5)". CLAUDE.md:472 records the local copy as identical to that commit (checked 2026-10-22), and `backlog/tasks/task-1292 - SAT-specification-from-thermo-nova-ground-truth-firmware-SAT-specification-and-a-detailed-comparison.md:24` records the method (`diff -rq`, only `__pycache__` differed). |
| OTGW firmware | branch `dev`, commit `51df6b486` | relative to `src/OTGW-firmware`, for example `SATcontrol.ino:1234` | Task definition. |

The area checks read the firmware working tree at `2c194a329`; the repository HEAD is now `3b41e0560`. `git diff --numstat 51df6b486 HEAD -- src/OTGW-firmware` shows that `SATcontrol.ino`, `SATpid.ino`, `SATcycles.ino` and `safeTimers.h` did not change. Every other changed file has as many added as removed lines (version banners and a default hostname), except `OTGW-firmware.h` (3 added, 2 removed). No area table row cites that file; the one citation of it, in the area 8 preamble, is given at `2c194a329`. The firmware line numbers therefore hold for `51df6b486`. Exception: the passages changed for TASK-1273 (executive summary item 3 and its phase-clock neighbour, the duty-formula row, the two area 4 rows on the boiler temperature, the area 4 row "PWM IDLE status", and the area 4 list items 2 and 3 and the unclear item 2) cite `SATcontrol.ino` as changed by that task, the working tree and not `51df6b486`. From line 729 of `SATcontrol.ino` on, the two numberings differ by up to 38 lines.

## How to read this document

- Every factual statement carries a citation `file:line` or `file:line-line`. Citations of `.py` files are thermo-nova; citations of `.ino`, `.h` and `.cpp` files are firmware. A few citations point to other repository files by their full path.
- "Derived" or "computed" marks a result worked out by reading or calculating from the cited code. It was not run on a device, in a simulator or in a test.
- Behaviour that the code does not show is never filled in. Each area ends with its own list "Not found in the code / unclear". The assembly adds a short list of its own at the very end.
- The area sections below are reproduced unchanged from the eight area comparisons, in area order. The executive summary and the totals are taken only from those area tables.

### Verdict legend

| Verdict | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **match** | Same behaviour in both code bases. |
| **differs** | Both have the feature, but it behaves differently. Partial matches are also "differs". |
| **missing in firmware** | thermo-nova has it, the firmware does not. |
| **firmware-only** | The firmware has it, thermo-nova does not. |

### Adversarial verification

After each area table was written, a separate verification pass reopened the cited lines in both code bases and tried to refute each verdict (another firmware call site, a dead or conditional thermo-nova path, a wrong citation). A verdict followed by "(verified)" held up in that pass. Of the 443 table rows:

- 434 rows are marked "(verified)".
- 1 row is marked "(corrected)": area 5, row 27. Its verdict was changed to "differs" in that pass.
- 8 rows carry no mark: area 8, rows 19, 20, 22, 44, 45, 56, 60 and 61. All eight are "match" rows that the area 8 pass did not reopen.

## Executive summary for Sergeant D

**Ranking rule.** First the differences that change what the boiler receives with default settings, then those that only act in a fault situation, then features that are off by default or only active with zones. Each item gives what thermo-nova does, what the firmware does, the citations on both sides, and where to find the full rows. All items come from the area tables below; none was added at assembly.

### What matches: the suppression sequence itself

With overshoot protection switched on in both, the formulas and defaults of the PWM setpoint sequence match (area 4, rows "Suppression: what it writes" to "Suppression setpoint: formula and default"). This is the part earlier AI work described wrongly. The edges differ: the clamp of the flame-off hold, the clamp of the suppressed setpoint, the allowed range of the suppression offset, when the stored hold is cleared, and what happens when the return or flow temperature is missing (area 4, rows "Flame-off hold: clamp", "Suppression offset: allowed range", "Suppressed setpoint: clamp", "When the stored hold is cleared", "Missing return or flow temperature in the override").

- Suppression writes only the control setpoint (CS), never a modulation value (heating_control.py:439-448; SATcontrol.ino:709-717).
- While PWM is on and no hot water is active, a gas boiler gets a maximum relative modulation (MM) of 0 in the ON and OFF phases (heating_control.py:517-518; SATcontrol.ino:308). During hot water the two differ (item 6).
- In the OFF phase, CS = 10 °C (heating_control.py:388-390; SATcontrol.ino:875-878).
- While waiting for the flame, CS = return temperature + 18 °C (heating_control.py:397-401; SATcontrol.ino:701-707).
- For 20 s after the flame lights, that value is held (heating_control.py:414-426; SATcontrol.ino:884-885).
- After the 20 s, CS = flow temperature - 1 °C (heating_control.py:440-441; SATcontrol.ino:713).

The PID core also matches: the error, the heating curve formula, the automatic gains, "integral only inside the 0.1 °C band", and the derivative sign, filter and cap (area 2, rows 1, 2, 6, 7, 14, 19 to 21). So does the duty-cycle formula (area 4, row "Duty-cycle formula": pwm.py:229-239, formula at pwm.py:238; SATcontrol.ino:729-756). Since TASK-1273 the boiler temperature fed into it follows thermo-nova as well, with narrow gaps (item 3).

### Differences, ranked by impact on heating behaviour

1. **Whether the suppression sequence runs at all (overshoot protection default).** In thermo-nova the switch is read from the entry data and is False when absent (entry_data.py:149-151). No configuration step sets it; only a migration from old options does (__init__.py:159-162). With it off, PWM leaves CS at its previous value and only sends MM = 0 (heating_control.py:385-386). The firmware has it on by default (SATtypes.h:488), so it sends the whole sequence above. Impact: large. Answered: Sergeant D chose on by default for all heating systems, a deliberate deviation from a fresh thermo-nova install (entry 20) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351). (Area 4, row "Default of overshoot protection"; area 8, row 49.)

2. **When the boiler counts as "no heat needed", and when CH is switched on.** thermo-nova uses 22 °C for every heating system: heater ON only when the requested setpoint is above 22 °C (heating_control.py:64-72, const.py:34), and CH on only when CS is above 22 °C (heating_control.py:238). The firmware switches the boiler off when the requested setpoint (PID output after the solar offset) is at or below 28.2 °C for radiators or 21 °C for underfloor: `CS=10`, `CH=0`, PWM left and reset, MM = the configured maximum (`satColdCutoff`, SATchEnable.h:26-29; SATcontrol.ino:130-131, SATcontrol.ino:339-343, SATcontrol.ino:5206-5214; `satPwmDisableOnHeaterOff`, SATcontrol.ino:1100-1109). CH follows `satChEnableDecide`: 0 at or below the cold setpoint or in the PWM OFF phase, otherwise 1, also in the PWM ON phase whatever CS keeps the flame on (SATchEnable.h:46-52, SATcontrol.ino:5223). Impact: with radiators, requests between 22 and 28.2 °C heat in thermo-nova and switch the boiler off in the firmware. Derived (target 20 °C, coefficient 2.0 in both, P, I and D zero): the firmware stops radiators above about 16 °C outside, thermo-nova never stops on the curve alone. The same threshold also gates the firmware's switch into PWM (SATcontrol.ino:5542-5546). **Decided (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351):** Sergeant D chose 28.2 / 21 instead of 22, everywhere thermo-nova uses `COLD_SETPOINT` (entries 7 and 11, answer 2); the PWM-ON CH rule is entry 9, confirmed on his installation in entry 17; the cutoff leaving PWM is entry 12. Still open: "below" in his words against the firmware's "at or below" (entry 7). (Area 1, row 22; area 2, row 47; area 3, "Heater on/off" rows; area 4, row "Heat-demand threshold"; area 5, row 34; area 8, rows 11 and 12.)

3. **How long the burner runs per PWM cycle (changed by TASK-1273, impact now low).** thermo-nova computes the duty cycle from the real flow temperature, at least base offset + 1 (pwm.py:232-235). Before TASK-1273 the firmware used a fixed base + 20 (47.2 °C for radiators), so requested 40 °C with the boiler at 60 °C gave a duty of 0.39 in thermo-nova and 0.64 in the firmware. Now the firmware does what thermo-nova does: BT is seeded with the flow temperature at the first flame, at the first ignition of an ON phase and at an ON start with the flame already burning; it is filtered 0.3 / 0.7 during the 180 s after that, held until the next ON phase seeds it, floored at base offset + 1 and cleared on reset (SATcontrol.ino:749-751, SATcontrol.ino:764-782, SATcontrol.ino:875-881, SATcontrol.ino:967; pwm.py:130-131, pwm.py:148-149, pwm.py:164-167, pwm.py:189-191, pwm.py:232-235, pwm.py:100). A relight after a mid-phase flame-out keeps BT and does not reopen the window, as in thermo-nova (SATcontrol.ino:778; pwm.py:145-146). Without a flow temperature the PWM state is reset and the continuous setpoint is used, as thermo-nova does when PWM goes IDLE (SATcontrol.ino:741-748, SATcontrol.ino:905-907; pwm.py:122-128). Sergeant D confirmed the 180 s window and chose the thermo-nova solution (`docs/sat/sergeant-d-corrections.md` entry 1). The same example now gives 0.39 in both (arithmetic: (40 - 27.2) / (60 - 27.2)). What remains, derived from the code and not run: the firmware filters on its control tick, 30 s by default and so about five samples in the window, while the thermo-nova control loop is scheduled every 5 s, so 36 runs fall in the 180 s window, more with other triggers (climate.py:706-710, climate.py:526-528; SATtypes.h:444); and a stint that starts in duty range 5 with the flame already lit is not reseeded, because range 5 sets the ON phase without the OFF-to-ON step that reseeds (SATcontrol.ino:797-802, SATcontrol.ino:875-881; pwm.py:164-167). (Area 4, rows "Boiler temperature in the duty formula before anything is learned" and "Learning the effective boiler temperature".)

4. **Start-up and switching SAT on: the firmware first commands "heating off".** thermo-nova runs its first pass about 1 s after start (climate.py:144, climate.py:530) and sends the curve plus P, I and D from the restored PID values (pid.py:126-132, pid.py:147-155). It also restores the last control setpoint and the PWM state (heating_control.py:186-187, pwm.py:111-115). The firmware sends `CS=0` at boot (SATcontrol.ino:3702-3710), waits one control interval, default 30 s (SATcontrol.ino:3719, SATtypes.h:444), and its first PID call returns 0 (SATpid.ino:264-280). That 0 is raised to 10 °C (SATcontrol.ino:4911), the cold cutoff applies, and `CS=10` with `CH=0` is sent (SATcontrol.ino:4971-4983). Impact (computed): in single-zone use, after every reboot and every runtime enable, the boiler is commanded off for one or two ticks (30 to 60 s at the default) after the first 30 s wait. Nothing of the setpoint or PWM state is restored. (Area 1, rows 4 to 8; area 2, row 28; area 4, row "Start-up and restart".)

5. **Falling demand with a hot boiler in continuous mode.** thermo-nova holds the setpoint at most at its previous value while the request falls: `max(requested, min(boiler - offset, previous))` (heating_control.py:485-489). The firmware has no previous value and always uses `max(pidOutput, flow - fFlowOffset)` (SATcontrol.ino:1179-1186). Example from the table: previous 40, request 38, flow 50, offset 2 gives 40 in thermo-nova and 48 in the firmware. Impact: the firmware can raise the setpoint while demand falls. (Area 1, row 23; area 3, row "Continuous rule: hold on a falling request"; area 4, row "Continuous-mode clamp"; area 8, row 42.)

6. **Hot water (DHW) while in PWM.** thermo-nova keeps writing during DHW and raises MM to the configured maximum (heating_control.py:136-137, heating_control.py:517-520). It also blocks the overshoot switch to PWM for 300 s after DHW ends (heating_control.py:268-274, const.py:19). During DHW the firmware tick returns before any CS, MM or CH command (SATcontrol.ino:4865-4882; line numbers refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351); a running PWM ON phase is paused for the draw and resumed after it (TASK-1422; Sergeant D entries 95, 103, 104; SATcontrol.ino:5181, :5190); it only sends `SW=` once on DHW entry when DHW control is on (SATcontrol.ino:4869-4878). So an MM of 0 from PWM stays on the boiler. Its 300 s guard exists but is never refreshed, because the code that refreshes it only runs on ticks without DHW (SATcycles.ino:964-972, SATcontrol.ino:4865-4882). Impact: after a draw, hot flow water left over from DHW can push the firmware into PWM; thermo-nova waits 300 s. What the boiler does with MM = 0 during a draw is in neither code. (Area 4, rows "MM during DHW in PWM" and "300 s guard after DHW"; area 5, row 42; area 8, rows 28 and 29.)

7. **No outside temperature.** thermo-nova sends nothing while the outside temperature is missing (climate.py:544-545). The firmware's OpenTherm outside value starts at 0.0 (OTGW-Core.h:82) and is used as a real value (SATcontrol.ino:1302). A real 0.0 °C from the boiler is also replaced by the weather value (SATcontrol.ino:1299-1300). Impact: without any outside source, the firmware heats all year on the curve for 0 °C outside (derived for radiators, target 20 °C, coefficient 1.5: 34.7 °C). (Area 1, row 29; area 3, row "Input: no outside temperature"; area 8, row 32.)

8. **How the firmware enters and leaves PWM, and how it times the phases.**
   - Overshoot margin: thermo-nova switches to PWM when flow is at least requested + 3.0 °C for 60 s (heating_control.py:288-291, cycles/const.py:10). The firmware uses more than requested + 2.0 °C (SATcycles.ino:973, SATtypes.h:454); a firmware comment says TASK-1268 lowered it on purpose (SATtypes.h:454).
   - Sustain counting: since TASK-1420 (entries 86, 95) all three sustained timers start at the first qualifying tick on the tick time (one `millis()` read per tick), as thermo-nova (helpers.py:51-56, heating_control.py:293-297, :342-345, :364-367): overshoot (SATcycles.ino:1043-1062), underheat and saturation (SATcycles.ino:1072-1104) share one helper (SATcycles.ino:152-160), and none stops above a 60 s tick. The control timer runs as SKIP_MISSED_TICKS (SATcontrol.ino:4277, settingStuff.ino:1005, safeTimers.h:131-135), so ticks are never closer than one interval and a timer fires on the first tick at least its length after the first qualifying tick (entry 109).
   - Cycle verdicts: thermo-nova also enables PWM after a continuous cycle classed as overshoot, and disables it after a PWM cycle classed as underheat (pwm.py:219-227, heating_control.py:167). The firmware has no such rule (area 4 searches for `satSetControlMode(`). Still true on HEAD (checked 2026-10-24, TASK-1351): the only PWM entries are the 60 s overshoot switch (SATcycles.ino:1001) and the manual command (SATcontrol.ino:2575) [lines refreshed 2026-10-25, TASK-1367 review], and a code comment states that `on_cycle_end` is not ported (SATcontrol.ino:1110-1111). Sergeant D decided to port both branches with margin 2.0 and without a user-setting gate (entries 15 and 18); that port (TASK-1331) is not in the tree.
   - Phase clock: thermo-nova times the ON phase from its start and restarts at ignition (pwm.py:162, pwm.py:186-193, pwm.py:205), and only starts an ON phase with an on-time of at least 180 s (pwm.py:155). The firmware times ON from the last real flame-on (SATcontrol.ino:834-837), ends the ON phase after 180 s without ignition (SATcontrol.ino:844-855), and has no minimum on-time check at the OFF-to-ON start (SATcontrol.ino:859-886). Derived, not run: if the flame has not lit by the next PWM check, the firmware can end the new ON phase at once. Since then shown on the host (`tests/test_sat_hp_pwm.py`, `test_r3_on_phase_waits_for_flame`, an expected failure): the ON phase ends 30 s after its start without a flame. Since TASK-1366 a heat pump in forced PWM at duty 0.05-0.95 (1800 s) takes this path too; before, duty at or above 0.5 was range 5, which skips the state machine. Queued as Q35 (timing) and Q38 (start gate).
   - Starts per hour: the firmware holds the next ON phase after 3 flame-ons in the last hour, DHW ignitions included (SATtypes.h:525, SATcontrol.ino:836-854, SATcycles.ino:723-724). thermo-nova compares its start count with `duty_cycle_seconds`, 780 by default, which an hour of PWM cannot reach (pwm.py:156, entry_data.py:220, const.py:167; derived).
   (Area 4, rows 33, 36, 39, 40, 45 to 49, 54; area 5, rows 40, 41, 44, 45.)

9. **The integral can build up twice as fast, and the firmware reacts more slowly.** thermo-nova runs its control pass every 5 s and its PID every 60 s (climate.py:706-716). The firmware runs one tick every 30 s by default (SATtypes.h:444). Both add a fixed 60 s step per PID update (pid.py:191; SATpid.ino:166), but the firmware updates on every tick where the error changed and about every 60 s otherwise (SATpid.ino:276-285). Impact (computed): while the room value keeps moving, the firmware integral grows up to 2 times faster at the 30 s default. (Area 1, rows 16 to 18; area 2, rows 12 and 13; area 8, row 6.)

10. **Room temperature lost: opposite reactions.** thermo-nova resets the PID but keeps sending, so the output stays curve plus the last P term (climate.py:468-482, pid.py:134-137, climate.py:544-545). The firmware sends no new values. On PIC boards the last `CS=` keeps being re-sent every 30 s until the trip (SATcontrol.ino:403-414, SATcontrol.ino:4608). After 10 invalid ticks in a row it trips, sends `CS=0` and stays off until SAT is enabled again or the device reboots (SATcontrol.ino:4669-4683, SATcontrol.ino:4580-4581). Impact: thermo-nova keeps heating, the firmware hands the boiler back to the thermostat after about 5 minutes at the default tick. Safety-relevant; Sergeant D to decide. (Area 1, row 30; area 3, row "Input: room sensor lost"; area 8, row 33.)

11. **Defaults that differ.**
    - Heating curve coefficient: now 2.0 in both (const.py:182; SATtypes.h:465, TASK-1347, entry 24); a coefficient a unit already stores is kept. Before TASK-1347 the firmware default was 1.5 (curve 34.7 °C against 37.2 °C, radiators, target 20 °C, outside 0 °C). (Corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351.)
    - Maximum flow setpoint: thermo-nova 55 °C (50 °C underfloor), user-settable (helpers.py:97-99, config_flow.py:612-614). The firmware uses the lowest of the boiler's MaxTSet, a system cap of 62 °C radiators, 45 °C underfloor, 40 °C heat pump, a hard ceiling and 65 °C (SATcontrol.ino:228-252), and has no user setting for it (SATcontrol.ino:248, SATtypes.h:514). When the boiler reports a MaxTSet between 30 °C and the system cap, the firmware's "full demand ends PWM" rule can never fire (SATcontrol.ino:4911-4920).
    - Cycles per hour 4 against 3 (const.py:119; SATtypes.h:525), so a default PWM period of 900 s against 1200 s.
    - Presets: comfort 20 against 21, sleep 15 against 16, away 10 against 15 °C, plus a firmware-only eco preset (const.py:175-179; SATtypes.h:446-451).
    - Window minimum open time 15 s against 60 s (const.py:172; SATtypes.h:464).
    (Area 2, row 4; area 3, rows "Curve: default coefficient" and "Requested setpoint: upper clamp"; area 4, row "Default cycles per hour"; area 6, rows 28 and 46; area 8, rows 38, 39, 41, 50 to 54.)

12. **Changing the target does not reset the firmware PID.** thermo-nova resets the main PID and the room PIDs (integral and raw derivative) on every target change (climate.py:641-660, climate.py:461-464). The firmware does not reset on a manual target change, and a preset change only zeroes the published copy of the integral, not the controller value (SATcontrol.ino:996-999, SATpid.ino:298). Impact: limited, because both zero the integral at the next update when the error is above 0.1 °C (pid.py:187-189, SATpid.ino:154-157); the old derivative keeps acting. (Area 2, rows 32 and 33; area 6, row 36.)

13. **Rooms or zones (only when the firmware has more than one zone; the default is one zone, SATtypes.h:531).** thermo-nova takes `max(main PID, rooms)` (climate.py:385-386). The rooms value is the 75 % point, capped at the highest room: `min(highest, baseline + 5.0)` (area.py:294-301). A room more than 0.3 °C too warm caps the whole setpoint at `22 + 10 - 4 x degrees over`, floor 22 (area.py:303-333, climate.py:388-389). The firmware replaces the main PID output with the zone result (SATcontrol.ino:4865-4867, SATcontrol.ino:4892), always adds the 5 °C (SATcontrol.ino:4881-4882), and only subtracts the degrees over (SATcontrol.ino:4884-4891). Example from the tables: rooms at 40 and 45 °C give 45 °C in thermo-nova and 50 °C in the firmware. With 4 zones the firmware picks the second highest zone (the assembly note at the end corrects one area 3 sentence on this). (Area 2, rows 38 to 41; area 3, "Requested setpoint" rows; area 6, rows 3, 12 to 18.)

14. **Solar gain compensation (off by default in both: const.py:132, SATtypes.h:516).** thermo-nova needs a sun elevation (solar_gain.py:56-58); the firmware works on indoor rise rate alone when no sun data is present (SATcontrol.ino:4468-4482 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]), so cooking or a stove can lower the setpoint, also at night. The firmware starts only after 600 s of all conditions (SATcontrol.ino:4489-4501 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]), where thermo-nova starts at once with a 600 s hold (solar_gain.py:60-63). Both now subtract the offset from the requested setpoint before the clamp and before PWM or continuous control (SATcontrol.ino:5137-5152; climate.py:391-394), and both freeze the integral only when the freeze option is on, skipping the whole update, reset included (SATpid.ino:157; climate.py:499-502, pid.py:167-168, pid.py:187-189). (Corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351: TASK-1323 changed both, entry 11.) (Area 7, rows S5, S10, S12, S15; area 2, row 16.)

15. **Gateway commands.**
    - Immergas: thermo-nova sends `TP=11:12=min(MM, 80)` and leaves `MM` uncapped (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:216-220). The firmware sends the flow setpoint in `TP=11:12` and caps `MM` at 80 (SATcontrol.ino:4984-4990, SATcontrol.ino:312). What the boiler does with either is in neither code. (Area 4, row "Immergas quirk"; area 8, rows 17 and 18.)
    - `PM=25` after every `CS=`: sent by thermo-nova (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:196-197), never by the firmware (area 8, row 9, search). Neither code says why.
    - Boot `PM=3`, `PM=15`, `PM=48`: thermo-nova sends them on every coordinator start (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:166-169). The firmware sends them only when SAT is enabled at boot (`pmChainStart()`, called only from `initSAT()`, SATcontrol.ino:3946-3948). On a PIC board they go out one at a time, because the PIC keeps one pending priority message: the next `PM=` is queued when the boiler answered the pending ID or after 60 s, and an ID that timed out is asked once more; on OT-Direct all three are queued at once (OTGW-Core.ino:3895-3982, TASK-1315). (Corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351: before TASK-1315 only `PM=48` survived the queue on a PIC.) (Area 1, rows 2 and 25; area 8, rows 3 and 9.)

### Firmware-only additions that act on the boiler

These have no thermo-nova counterpart. They are listed so they can be confirmed, not ranked.

- `CS=0` at boot and on disable, which hands the boiler back to the thermostat (SATcontrol.ino:3702-3710, SATcontrol.ino:2175-2179). thermo-nova leaves its last commands in place when switched off (climate.py:544-545). (Area 1, rows 4 and 28; area 8, rows 2 and 31.)
- Safety trip after 10 invalid room readings or 5 ticks without a command interface (SATcontrol.ino:4676-4682, SATcontrol.ino:4996-5005). (Area 8, row 35.)
- No SAT commands while the boiler reports a fault (SATcontrol.ino:4716-4719). (Area 1, row 34; area 8, row 34.)
- MQTT-loss fallback, and a fixed 45 °C safe setpoint after 2 h without room data in that fallback (SATcontrol.ino:4540-4563, SATcontrol.ino:4730-4746). (Area 8, row 36.)
- A 45 s low-pass filter on the room temperature before the PID (SATcontrol.ino:4694-4713). (Area 1, row 32; area 2, row 25.)
- Summer mode that turns heating off after warm hours outside, off by default (SATcontrol.ino:4764-4776, SATtypes.h:499). It shares the "summer simmer" name with thermo-nova's comfort index, which is only an index (summer_simmer.py:26-35). (Area 7, row T10.)

### Firmware defects found that do not change heating today

- Tail percentile sort: `int8_t j` goes negative for ring index 129 and above, and the sort then writes outside its array (SATcycles.ino:525-533). Read, not compiled or run. It corrupts cycle class labels today and would matter if cycle-based PWM switching (item 8) were ported. (Area 5, row 25.)
- Off-time per cycle is always 0, so the 4 h and 24 h duty ratios are always 1.0 (SATcycles.ino:760, SATcycles.ino:588-596). (Area 5, row 36.)
- Fixed in TASK-1323: the solar "freeze integral" setting was stored but never read; the PID now reads it (SATpid.ino:157, SATtypes.h:549) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351). (Area 2, row 18; area 7, row S16.)
- ECO heating mode is stored and published but has no effect on control (SATtypes.h:524, SATcontrol.ino:2533, SATcontrol.ino:2978). In thermo-nova ECO uses only the main PID (climate.py:377-379). (Area 2, row 44; area 6, row 19.)

## Totals per area

Counts of table rows per verdict, taken from the area tables below. These are table rows, not distinct behaviours: some differences, such as the cold setpoint, the curve coefficient default, the maximum setpoint, the tick rate and the continuous clamp, appear in several areas and are counted once in each. The same behaviour can also carry a different verdict word in different areas, for example the continuous-mode hold (area 3 "missing in firmware", area 8 row 42 "differs") and the `MI=500` boot command (area 1 row 3 "match", area 8 row 4 "differs").

| Area | match | differs | missing in firmware | firmware-only | Rows | Marked (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Start-up sequence and control loop timing | 6 | 23 | 4 | 6 | 39 | 39 |
| 2. PID controller | 9 | 33 | 3 | 5 | 50 | 50 |
| 3. Heating curve and setpoint selection | 9 | 38 | 5 | 10 | 62 | 62 |
| 4. PWM, modulation and the suppression mechanism | 21 | 37 | 2 | 7 | 67 | 67 |
| 5. Boiler state, flame and cycle tracking | 6 | 33 | 6 | 4 | 49 | 48 (row 27 "corrected") |
| 6. Areas, presets, window detection and thermostat sync | 6 | 43 | 4 | 5 | 58 | 58 |
| 7. Solar gain, summer simmer, temperature history and health sensors | 11 | 37 | 3 | 6 | 57 | 57 |
| 8. Boiler I/O, DHW, limits and safety, configuration defaults | 12 | 36 | 4 | 9 | 61 | 53 (8 match rows unmarked) |
| **Total** | **80** | **280** | **31** | **52** | **443** | **434** |

Area 7's own "Verdict counts" table (inside its section) gives the same figures as this table: 11, 37, 3, 6, total 57.

---

## Start-up sequence and control loop timing

thermo-nova (ground truth) citations are relative to `custom_components/sat`; firmware citations are relative to `src/OTGW-firmware` at `51df6b486`. Rows are in time order. Sources, scope and verdict words are explained below the table.

| Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. When control starts | On a normal Home Assistant boot, four steps wait for the "HA started" event: compute the curves, start the timers, run the first PID update, schedule the first pass (`climate.py:139-144`). On a reload they run at once (`climate.py:133-138`). | `initSAT()` runs once, at the end of `setup()` (`OTGW-firmware.ino:567`), after the WiFi start, which may hold a configuration portal for up to 240 s (`OTGW-firmware.ino:356`). | differs (verified) | Both start control after their host is up, by different mechanisms: thermo-nova has an event-gated path and an immediate reload path, the firmware has one path behind the WiFi start. The firmware then waits one more control interval before its first tick (row 5). | None for the gate itself. See rows 5 and 6. |
| 2. Boot commands `PM=3`, `PM=15`, `PM=48` | Sent every time the MQTT coordinator is added to HA (`coordinator/mqtt/__init__.py:62`, `coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:166-169`). That happens twice per start (`__init__.py:51`, `climate.py:147`) and does not depend on the HVAC mode. | Sent once, in `initSAT()`, only when SAT is enabled and a command interface exists (`SATcontrol.ino:3721-3728`). Not retried later, and not sent when SAT is enabled at runtime, because only `initSAT()` sends them (grep for `PM=` over the firmware finds only these three commands plus a comment in `OTDirect.ino:3369`). | differs (verified) | Same commands. In the firmware they are skipped for the whole session when SAT is off at boot, or when no interface existed at boot. The firmware's OT-Direct command handler reads `PM=<id>` as "priority message: request this MsgID on the next cycle" (`OTDirect.ino:3369-3392`); MsgIDs 3, 15 and 48 are the slave configuration and member id, the boiler capacity and minimum modulation, and the DHW setpoint bounds (`OTGW-Core.h:377`, `OTGW-Core.h:389`, `OTGW-Core.h:422`). What the PIC does with them, and why thermo-nova sends them, is not in either code base. | Send the boot set also on the first enabled tick when it was not sent in `initSAT()`. Ask Sergeant D whether sending them twice, as thermo-nova does, matters. |
| 3. `MI=500` for Ideal, Intergas, Nefit | Sent in the same boot step when the manufacturer is one of these three (`coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:171-172`). At boot that is only the configured manufacturer; the fallback from the boiler member id is filled in only by a control pass (`coordinator/__init__.py:107-108`, `coordinator/__init__.py:345-351`). | Sent when the effective manufacturer has the `MI_500` quirk (`SATcontrol.ino:3729-3733`). With the setting on AUTO the effective manufacturer is the detected one (`SATcontrol.ino:144-149`), which starts as OTHER (`SATtypes.h:240`); the default setting is AUTO (`SATtypes.h:472`), and only Ideal, Intergas and Nefit carry the quirk (`SATcontrol.ino:128-132`). thermo-nova's `manufacturer` property returns only the stored value, with no member-id lookup (`coordinator/__init__.py:146-148`). | match (verified) | In practice both send it at boot only when the user configured one of these three manufacturers. Whether the firmware can already know the manufacturer from an OT frame at `initSAT()` is open (last list). | None. |
| 4. Boot `CS=0` release | No `CS=` at start. The first `CS=` comes with the first control pass (`heating_control.py:236`, `coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:195-197`). | Sends `CS=0` in `initSAT()` whenever a command interface exists, even when SAT is disabled (`SATcontrol.ino:3702-3710`). If there was no interface yet, it is sent on the first enabled pass that has one (`SATcontrol.ino:4585-4589`). | firmware-only (verified) | A firmware comment states the intent: `CS=0` releases a control setpoint the PIC may still hold from before the reboot, "so the thermostat controls the boiler" until SAT's first tick (`SATcontrol.ino:3702-3705`). What the gateway and boiler then do is outside the code. | Keep, and document it as a firmware addition. Confirm with Sergeant D. |
| 5. Time from start to the first control pass | The first pass is scheduled 1 s after the start step (`climate.py:144`, `climate.py:530`). | The first tick comes one control interval after `initSAT()` restarts the timer: default 30 s, allowed 10 to 300 s (`SATcontrol.ino:3719`, `safeTimers.h:131-135`, `SATtypes.h:444`, `settingStuff.ino:1001`). | differs (verified) | About 29 s later at the default interval. In that gap the last SAT command is the boot `CS=0` (row 4). | Covered by row 6. Optionally run the first tick right after `initSAT()`. |
| 6. Value of the first setpoint sent | The first pass sends the PID output when a curve exists (`climate.py:369-394`). If the first PID update was skipped, that output still uses the restored error, integral and derivative (`pid.py:96-132`). Without a curve it sends 10 °C (`climate.py:369-394`). Continuous mode uses the restored control setpoint as "previous" (`heating_control.py:454`). | The first PID call sets its reference values and returns the stored output (`SATpid.ino:264-280`), which is 0 after the reset in `initSAT()` (`SATpid.ino:109`, `SATcontrol.ino:3683`). The output is only written by a call that computes (`SATpid.ino:302-317`). With the default single zone (`SATtypes.h:531`; the multi-zone override at `SATcontrol.ino:4846-4899` runs only above one zone), the tick raises 0 to 10 °C (`SATcontrol.ino:4911`). The cold cutoff then forces 10 °C and MM = the configured maximum (`SATcontrol.ino:5206-5214`, `SATcontrol.ino:1108`), and `CH=0` follows (`satChEnableDecide`, `SATcontrol.ino:5223`) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351). A steady room repeats this on the next ticks until 60 s have passed since the first call, because the PID skips when the error is unchanged and less than 60 s passed (`SATpid.ino:276-285`). | differs (verified) | After every reboot, and after every runtime enable, the boiler receives a heating-off command (`CS=10`, `CH=0`) from the first tick until the PID first computes: one or two ticks at the default 30 s interval (up to 60 s), after the 30 s wait of row 5 (at runtime enable, after the wait of row 28). With more than one zone the zone outputs replace the PID output, and that path was not traced (last list). thermo-nova's first pass, about 1 s after start, sends the curve plus P, I and D. Those terms come from the restored PID values, after the first `control_pid` has already applied the live error (`climate.py:143-144`, `pid.py:126-132`). The restored integral survives only when the room is within 0.1 °C of target (row 12). | Let the first PID call compute an output at once (or build it from the restored terms), so the first tick sends a real setpoint. Confirm with Sergeant D that resuming at once is the intended design. |
| 7. Control setpoint restored | Restored from the saved climate attribute `setpoint` (`heating_control.py:186-187`). | Not restored. The last control setpoint starts at 0 (`SATcontrol.ino:693`) and is set to 0 again on disable (`SATcontrol.ino:2166`), and the command cache starts empty (`SATcontrol.ino:3700`, `SATcontrol.ino:356-362`). | missing in firmware (verified) | thermo-nova can resend the previous setpoint in several PWM cases, and uses it as "previous" in the continuous clamp. The firmware starts from nothing. | Handle it together with row 6. |
| 8. PWM state restored | The PWM "enabled" flag is restored when it was true (`pwm.py:111-115`). The PWM status starts at IDLE (`pwm.py:63`, `pwm.py:92`). | Control mode always starts as CONTINUOUS when SAT is enabled (`SATcontrol.ino:3721-3722`, `SATcontrol.ino:4617-4619`). PWM is set again by the force-PWM setting (`SATcontrol.ino:4923-4925`), by the auto-switch check (`SATcontrol.ino:4951-4953`, `SATcycles.ino:978-980`), or by a user mode command (`SATcontrol.ino:2242-2244`). That command is kept only in the transient `state.sat.iRequestedControlMode` (`SATtypes.h:208`, `SATcontrol.ino:2243`); a grep finds no other write and no save of it (`SATcontrol.ino:2240-2246`, `SATcontrol.ino:2628`), so it is not restored either. | missing in firmware (verified) | A boiler that ran in PWM before the restart runs continuously in the firmware until the auto-switch decides again. | Ask Sergeant D whether the mode should survive a restart. If yes, persist it. |
| 9. On/off state restored (HVAC mode versus SAT enabled) | The HVAC mode is restored from the previous state (`climate.py:184-185`). A fresh install starts OFF (`climate.py:209-210`). | `SATenabled` is a saved setting (`settingStuff.ino:315`), default false (`SATtypes.h:437`). | match (verified) | Both come back in the same on/off state after a restart. | None. |
| 10. Target temperature restored | Restored from the saved attribute (`climate.py:176-182`). If missing, HA's `min_temp` is used (`climate.py:177-180`). | `fTargetTemp` is a saved setting (`settingStuff.ino:319`), default 20 °C (`SATtypes.h:441`). | match (verified) | Same after a restart. The fresh-install defaults cannot be compared, because `min_temp` comes from the HA base class, not from SAT code. | None. |
| 11. PID state restored | Always restored, with no age check: last error, last temperature, time of the last derivative update, integral and raw derivative (`pid.py:147-155`). | Only the integral and the raw derivative (plus two display fields) are restored (`SATcontrol.ino:2038-2042`, `SATpid.ino:75-80`). This happens only when the clock is NTP-synced and the file is at most 1800 s old (`SATcontrol.ino:2026-2037`, `SATcontrol.ino:1993`). The restore then runs once more, on the first main-loop pass where the clock is synced, whether or not the boot restore worked and also while SAT is disabled, because it sits before the enable check (`SATcontrol.ino:4515-4519`, `SATcontrol.ino:4568`, `OTGW-firmware.ino:1157`). | differs (verified) | After more than 30 minutes without power, the firmware starts with integral 0, while thermo-nova keeps it. The effect is limited, because both sides zero the integral on the first update when the error is above 0.1 °C (row 12). When the clock syncs late, the second restore can overwrite an integral the PID has already computed in this session. | Ask Sergeant D whether a maximum age is wanted. Do not let the NTP retry overwrite an integral that has already been computed. |
| 12. Restored integral at the first update | Set to 0 when the error is above 0.1 °C (`pid.py:187-189`, `const.py:16`). | Set to 0 when the error is above the deadband, default 0.1 °C (`SATpid.ino:154-157`, `SATtypes.h:443`). | match (verified) | Same rule. | None. |
| 13. First derivative after a restart | The restored last temperature and time are used, so the first slope can span the whole downtime. The filter factor is then close to 1 (`pid.py:151-152`, `pid.py:215-229`). | The reference temperature and time are taken from the first live reading (`SATpid.ino:264-273`). The restored raw derivative stays in use until a slope over more than 60 s of new data exists (`SATpid.ino:199-219`). | differs (verified) | The firmware does not compute a slope across the outage; thermo-nova can. Small effect on the D term in the first minutes. | Document. Ask Sergeant D which one is intended. |
| 14. When the PID state is saved | After every PID update, so about every 60 s (`pid.py:179-181`). A reset is not saved (`pid.py:134-139`). | Every 300 s, on a tick (`SATcontrol.ino:5010-5012`). A disable saves the zeroed state (`SATcontrol.ino:2170-2171`). | differs (verified) | After a reboot the firmware file can be up to 5 minutes old. After a disable followed by a reboot, the firmware restores zeros. In thermo-nova the reset itself is not saved, but its 60 s PID update keeps running and saving while the mode is OFF, because `control_pid` has no HVAC-mode check (`climate.py:466-502`, `climate.py:712-716`), so the store soon holds the state of the latest update. | Low priority. Document. |
| 15. Boiler data restored | MQTT gateways reload the last boiler values from storage (`coordinator/mqtt/__init__.py:49-50`, `coordinator/mqtt/__init__.py:71-74`). The first pass can therefore use the flame state and flow temperature from before the restart. | Not restored. Room temperature starts as NAN, flow and outside temperature as 0.0, `MaxTSet` as 0.0 (`OTGW-Core.h:79-82`, `OTGW-Core.h:103`). Only decoded OT frames fill them. | missing in firmware (verified) | The firmware uses only live boiler data. That avoids decisions based on stale values; thermo-nova can act on old data in its first pass. | Keep the firmware behaviour. Document it as a deliberate difference. |
| 16. Control tick period | A pass is scheduled every 5 s and runs 1 s after scheduling (`climate.py:706-710`, `climate.py:526-531`). | One control tick every `iControlInterval` seconds: default 30, allowed 10 to 300 (`SATcontrol.ino:4614`, `SATtypes.h:444`, `settingStuff.ino:1001`). | differs (verified) | The firmware reacts up to about 30 s later to new boiler data and to a new PID output. | Ask Sergeant D whether 30 s is acceptable, or lower the default. |
| 17. PID update timing | Own timer, every 60 s (`climate.py:712-716`, `pid.py:20`). The update runs on each timer tick when a curve and a temperature exist (`pid.py:159-163`). | Runs inside the control tick (`SATcontrol.ino:4839`). It computes only when at least 10 s passed and either the error changed by 0.001 °C or more, or at least 60 s passed (`SATpid.ino:21-22`, `SATpid.ino:276-285`). It is the only call site (`SATcontrol.ino:4839`); in thermo-nova `control_pid` is called only at start-up and by its timer (`climate.py:137`, `climate.py:143`, `climate.py:714`). | differs (verified) | With a moving error the firmware updates the PID every tick, so every 30 s by default. With a steady error it updates about every 60 s. thermo-nova always updates every 60 s. | Run the firmware PID on a fixed 60 s cadence, like thermo-nova. See row 18. |
| 18. Integral step per update | Adds `ki * error * 60` per update, a fixed 60 s step (`pid.py:191`), on the 60 s timer (row 17). | Also adds `ki * error * 60` per update, a fixed 60 s step (`SATpid.ino:166`), but on the cadence of row 17. | differs (verified) | With a steady error both integrate at the same rate: one step about every 60 s. While the room value keeps moving from tick to tick, the firmware adds a step every tick, so its integral builds up to twice as fast as thermo-nova at the 30 s default and up to six times as fast at 10 s. At intervals above 60 s it builds more slowly. The 45 s room filter (row 32) may make small tick-to-tick changes common; that is a possibility, not a measured result. | Fix together with row 17: a 60 s PID cadence, or a step equal to the real elapsed time. Confirm with Sergeant D. |
| 19. When the heating curve is recomputed | At start-up and on events only: an inside or outside sensor change (`climate.py:767-773`), a target change (`climate.py:663`) or a room change (`climate.py:796`). Not on the periodic ticks. The value is rounded to 0.1 °C (`heating_curve.py:39`). | On every tick, from that tick's target and outside temperature (`SATcontrol.ino:4836`). Not rounded (`SATcontrol.ino:489-496`). | differs (verified) | Practically the same input. The firmware picks up an outside change at the next tick, thermo-nova at the sensor event. The rounding difference is at most 0.05 °C. | None. Document. |
| 20. Heating curve coefficient default | 2.0 (`const.py:182`). | 2.0 since TASK-1347 (`SATtypes.h:465`); a stored value is kept (entry 24). Before that 1.5. (Corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351; the rest of this row describes the old 1.5 default.) | match (corrected) | None for a fresh unit. A unit that already stores 1.5 keeps it (entry 24): its curve stays 25 % flatter (factor coefficient / 4: 0.375 instead of 0.5) and its automatic gains lower. | Done (TASK-1347, entry 24). |
| 21. Upper limit on the requested setpoint | The configured maximum setpoint, default 55 °C for radiators and 50 °C for underfloor (`entry_data.py:227-239`, `helpers.py:97-99`). The requested setpoint is clamped to it (`climate.py:394`, `coordinator/__init__.py:275-283`). | The lowest of: the boiler's `MaxTSet` (75 °C while it is below 30, which includes boot), a system cap (62 radiators, 45 underfloor, 40 heat pump), a hard cap (80 / 50) and `fMaxSetpoint` (default 65) (`SATcontrol.ino:228-252`, `SATtypes.h:514`, `OTGW-Core.h:103`). | differs (verified) | With defaults, the firmware can send up to 62 °C to radiators where thermo-nova stops at 55 °C. | Belongs to the setpoint-limits area. Ask Sergeant D which default is right. |
| 22. Heater on/off threshold and `CH=` | The heater is ON when the requested setpoint is above `COLD_SETPOINT`, 22 °C (`heating_control.py:64-72`, `const.py:34`). Otherwise the setpoint becomes 10 °C (`heating_control.py:229-234`). `CH=1` when the control setpoint is above 22 °C (`heating_control.py:238`). | Cold cutoff: when the requested setpoint (PID output after the solar offset) is at or below 28.2 °C (radiators) or 21 °C (underfloor), the setpoint becomes 10 °C, PWM is left and reset, and the modulation is the configured maximum, as thermo-nova's heater-off branch (`satColdCutoff`, `SATchEnable.h:26-29`; `SATcontrol.ino:5206-5214`; `satPwmDisableOnHeaterOff`, `SATcontrol.ino:1100-1109`). `CH=` comes from `satChEnableDecide`: 0 at or below the cold setpoint or when the PWM OFF phase drives the CS (overshoot protection on, no DHW, flow temperature known; `satPwmDrivesSetpoint`, `SATcontrol.ino:1010-1014`), else 1 (`SATchEnable.h:46-52`, `SATcontrol.ino:5223`). The threshold follows the heating system (`SATcontrol.ino:130-131`, `SATcontrol.ino:339-343`); thermo-nova has one value for all systems (`const.py:34`). (Corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351: was "below", "modulation 100" and "CH=1 above 10 °C"; TASK-1316, TASK-1323, TASK-1327.) | differs (verified) | For radiators, the firmware stops heating for requests between 22 and 28.2 °C that thermo-nova still sends. On mild days it switches the boiler off earlier. In the PWM ON phase the firmware keeps CH=1 whatever CS keeps the flame on; thermo-nova would clear CH for a CS at or below 22 °C. | Answered: 28.2 / 21 (entries 7 and 11), CH=1 in the PWM ON phase (entries 9 and 17), cutoff leaves PWM (entry 12). Open: "below" against "at or below" (entry 7). |
| 23. Continuous-mode clamp | Uses the previous control setpoint. When the request falls and the boiler is hot, the setpoint becomes `max(requested, min(boiler - offset, previous))` (`heating_control.py:450-489`). The setpoint therefore never rises above the previous value while the request falls. | No previous value: `max(pidOutput, boiler - offset)` when the flame is on and the boiler is hotter than the request (`SATcontrol.ino:1148-1187`). | differs (verified) | When the request falls and the previous setpoint was below `boiler - offset`, the firmware raises the setpoint to `boiler - offset`, where thermo-nova holds the lower previous value. That can give more heat than intended. This row also belongs to the continuous-mode area. | Add the previous-setpoint branch, following `heating_control.py:486-489`. |
| 24. When commands are sent | On every pass, with no change detection: `CS=`, `PM=25`, `MM=`, `CH=` (`heating_control.py:236-240`, `coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:195-197`, `coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:211-222`). The publish helper sends each command as given, with a 0.2 s pause after each (`coordinator/mqtt/__init__.py:134-147`). | Write-on-change, plus a resend of each command after 300 s (`SATcontrol.ino:351`, `SATcontrol.ino:383-396`, `SATcontrol.ino:4978-4983`). The cache is cleared at boot, on disable and when the OT bus comes back online (`SATcontrol.ino:3700`, `SATcontrol.ino:2182`, `SATcontrol.ino:4531-4534`). | differs (verified) | The boiler gets the same values, with far fewer commands on the bus. | Keep. Document. |
| 25. `PM=25` after every `CS=` | Sent after every `CS=` (`coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:197`). | Never sent: a grep for `PM=` over the firmware sources finds only the three boot commands (`SATcontrol.ino:3726-3728`) and a comment in the OT-Direct handler (`OTDirect.ino:3369`). | missing in firmware (verified) | The firmware's OT-Direct command handler reads `PM=25` as "request MsgID 25 on the next cycle" (`OTDirect.ino:3369-3392`); MsgID 25 is the boiler water temperature (`OTGW-Core.h:399`). What the PIC does with it, and why thermo-nova asks for it after every `CS=`, is not in either code base. | Ask Sergeant D or Schelte Bron what `PM=25` is for, then decide. |
| 26. PIC CS keepalive | None. | On PIC boards, once a `CS=` of 8 °C or more was sent, it is resent every 30 s (`SATcontrol.ino:403-414`). A firmware comment states the reason: the PIC drops such a CS after about 63 to 126 s without a new one (`SATcontrol.ino:398-402`). | firmware-only (verified) | Needed because of row 24: thermo-nova resends every pass anyway. | Keep. Document it as a firmware addition. |
| 27. Target temperature change | Resets the main PID and the room PIDs, recomputes the curves and schedules a pass within 1 s (`climate.py:641-667`, `climate.py:461-464`). | Stores the value only; the next tick reads it (`settingStuff.ino:997`, `SATcontrol.ino:1903-1934`). If the value equals a preset, the preset is activated, which zeroes only the displayed integral `fPidI` (`SATcontrol.ino:990-1007`); the PID itself keeps its own `_pid_integral` (`SATpid.ino:298`). | differs (verified) | The firmware reacts up to one interval later. The raw derivative is not reset. The integral is zeroed anyway at the next update if the new error is above 0.1 °C (row 12). | Reset the PID on a target change and run a tick soon after. Confirm with Sergeant D. |
| 28. Switching off and on | OFF or HEAT resets the PIDs and schedules a pass (`climate.py:570-602`). In OFF no command is sent at all (`climate.py:544-545`). | Disable: mode OFF, PID reset and saved as zeros, `CS=0` sent (`SATcontrol.ino:2160-2183`). Enable: no boot commands (`settingStuff.ino:981-993`, `SATcontrol.ino:1941-1957`). While SAT is off the loop returns before the timer check, so the timer's due time is not moved on (`SATcontrol.ino:4568-4573`, `SATcontrol.ino:4614`). `initSAT()` leaves the timer in skip-missed-ticks mode, because its one-argument call sets the type to 0 (`SATcontrol.ino:3719`, `safeTimers.h:96`, `safeTimers.h:131-135`, `safeTimers.h:247-258`). On enable, if the due time is at most 10 intervals in the past, the next pass fires one tick at once (`safeTimers.h:181`, `safeTimers.h:218-219`). If it is further behind, the timer is reset and the first tick comes one interval after the enable (`safeTimers.h:186-189`). Then the first-call behaviour of row 6 applies again. | differs (verified) | On disable the firmware sends `CS=0`; a firmware comment states the intent, so that "the physical thermostat resumes authority" (`SATcontrol.ino:2175-2178`). thermo-nova sends nothing more after OFF, so the last `CS=` and `CH=` it sent stay the last commands. What the gateway then does is outside the code. | Keep the firmware `CS=0`. Document it. Fix the heating-off start through row 6. |
| 29. No outside temperature | No pass sends anything while the outside temperature is missing (`climate.py:544-545`). The curve stays empty at start-up (`climate.py:693-694`). | The OT outside value starts as 0.0 (`OTGW-Core.h:82`) and is used as 0 °C when no other source exists (`SATcontrol.ino:1302`). A weather value replaces it only when the OT value is exactly 0.0 (`SATcontrol.ino:1299-1300`). An external outdoor value, when fresh, comes first (`SATcontrol.ino:1289-1297`). | differs (verified) | Without an outside sensor the firmware heats on the curve for 0 °C. thermo-nova does not control at all. | Treat "never received" as missing, as the firmware already does for the room temperature (NAN). Confirm with Sergeant D. |
| 30. No inside temperature | `control_pid` skips (`climate.py:480-482`), but the pass still sends a setpoint built from the previous PID terms (`pid.py:96-132`). | The tick stops before the PID and sends no new values. After 10 such ticks in a row SAT trips, disables itself and sends `CS=0` (`SATcontrol.ino:4669-4683`, `SATcontrol.ino:68`, `SATcontrol.ino:2179`). On PIC boards the keepalive runs on every pass, before the tick check (`SATcontrol.ino:4608`, `SATcontrol.ino:4614`), so the last `CS=` keeps being resent until the trip. | differs (verified) | thermo-nova keeps sending a setpoint from the last PID terms, with no time limit in this path. The firmware keeps its last values for about 5 minutes at the default interval. After that the firmware sends `CS=0`. | Keep. Document the safety trip as a firmware addition. |
| 31. Stale inside sensor | When the sensor is older than `sensor_max_value_age` (default 6 h), each 60 s PID run resets the PID and returns (`climate.py:470-478`, `const.py:170`). The stale value also reads as missing (`climate.py:677-681`). Passes keep sending, because the pass does not check the inside sensor (`climate.py:544-545`). | Each source has its own age limit: BLE 300 s, thermostat `Tr` 300 s (only once a thermostat has reported it), MQTT external 21600 s (`SATcontrol.ino:65-66`, `SATtypes.h:515`, `SATcontrol.ino:1192-1280`). A stale source falls through to the next source, or to NAN, which then follows row 30. | differs (verified) | thermo-nova keeps heating with the PID reset. The firmware switches source or ends in the safety trip. | Document. Ask Sergeant D which is intended. |
| 32. Room temperature filter | None in SAT. The HA sensor value is used directly (`climate.py:270-279`). | Low-pass filter with a 45 s time constant, seeded with the first valid reading (`SATcontrol.ino:4694-4713`). A grep of thermo-nova for filter or smoothing code finds only a boiler-pressure filter (`binary_sensor.py:315-323`). | firmware-only (verified) | Smoother input for the PID, and a short delay after a real temperature step. | Keep. Document. Confirm with Sergeant D. |
| 33. Hot water (DHW) during a tick | The pass still runs and sends `CS=`, `MM=`, `CH=` (`heating_control.py:197-240`). DHW sets the modulation state to HOT_WATER, so the maximum modulation is sent (`heating_control.py:136-137`, `heating_control.py:517-520`). In PWM mode the PWM setpoint step is skipped, so the previous control setpoint is sent again (`heating_control.py:385-386`). DHW also blocks the switch to PWM on overshoot (`heating_control.py:268-270`). In continuous mode the setpoint is computed as usual (`heating_control.py:450-489`). The 60 s PID update is not affected (`climate.py:466-502`). | The tick stops before the PID and sends no `CS=`, `MM=` or `CH=`. It sends `SW=` once on DHW entry when DHW control is on (`SATcontrol.ino:4851-4883`; line numbers refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351). | differs (verified) | During hot water the firmware leaves the last heating commands in place and does not update the PID. thermo-nova keeps updating the PID and, in continuous mode, the setpoint. | Compare in the DHW area. |
| 34. Boiler fault flag | No check in the pass (`climate.py:540-555`, `heating_control.py:197-240`). | The tick stops when slave status bit 0 is set (`SATcontrol.ino:4716-4719`). | firmware-only (verified) | No SAT commands while the boiler reports a fault. | Keep. Document. |
| 35. No command interface | Not applicable. Commands go to the coordinator (`heating_control.py:236-240`). | Each tick without an interface counts a failure. After 5, SAT trips and disables itself (`SATcontrol.ino:4996-5004`, `SATcontrol.ino:69`). | firmware-only (verified) | Stops SAT when the PIC or OT-Direct link is missing. | Keep. Document. |
| 36. MQTT fallback | None. | When SAT is disabled and MQTT has been lost for more than 300 s, SAT enables itself in memory. It disables again when MQTT returns (`SATcontrol.ino:4540-4563`). | firmware-only (verified) | The boiler stays heated when the outside controller is unreachable. | Keep. Document. |
| 37. Flame already burning at start | The first boiler update with the flame on counts as a flame start, for the device tracker and for the cycle tracker (`device/__init__.py:196-202`, `cycles/tracker.py:57-69`). | The previous flame state starts as false (`SATcontrol.ino:473`), so the first pass that sees the flame bit counts a new flame-on (`SATcontrol.ino:4592-4596`, `SATcycles.ino:701-714`). This edge check runs only while SAT is enabled (`SATcontrol.ino:4568`). | match (verified) | On both sides the first flame-on seen after start counts as a new flame start and cycle start, not the real ignition. Whether the PWM suppression delay is timed from this moment was not checked here; that belongs to the PWM area. | None. |
| 38. Flame-off time at start | The flame-off time starts empty (`device/__init__.py:32-33`), and so does the cycle tracker's (`cycles/tracker.py:42`). | Both flame-off timestamps are set to the boot moment (`SATcycles.ino:192`, `SATcycles.ino:247`, both inside `satCycleInit()` at `SATcycles.ino:188-258`). The first is used for the PWM OFF wait (`SATcontrol.ino:836-837`) and for the "stuck off" flame status after 5 minutes without flame (`SATcontrol.ino:3661-3665`). The second gives the off-with-demand time at the first flame-on (`SATcycles.ino:707`). | differs (verified) | It can change how long the first PWM OFF phase lasts, and the firmware can report "stuck off" 5 minutes after boot. The exact effect on the first PWM cycle is not worked out (last list). | Check in the PWM area. |
| 39. Fresh install | Starts OFF, so no control commands. Only the boot commands are sent (`climate.py:209-210`, `climate.py:544-545`, `coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:166-172`). | SAT starts disabled (`SATtypes.h:437`), so no control commands. Only the boot `CS=0` is sent (`SATcontrol.ino:3702-3710`). | match (verified) | No control until the user switches SAT on. The boot commands differ, as described in rows 2 and 4. | None. |

### The most important differences, in plain language

1. **After every restart the firmware first commands "heating off" for a while.** At boot it sends `CS=0`; a firmware comment says this is so the thermostat controls the boiler (`SATcontrol.ino:3702-3710`). About 30 s later the first tick sends "10 °C, heating off" (`CS=10`, `CH=0`), because, with one zone and the default 30 s interval, the PID only reports a real value from the second or third tick (`SATpid.ino:264-285`, `SATcontrol.ino:4971-4983`). thermo-nova's first pass, about 1 s after start, already sends the heating curve plus P, I and D, built from the saved PID values and the live error (`climate.py:143-144`, `climate.py:530`, `pid.py:126-132`). The same heating-off start happens when SAT is switched on at runtime. (Rows 4 to 7.)
2. **The firmware runs its control step six times less often.** thermo-nova runs a control pass every 5 s (`climate.py:706-710`). The firmware runs one tick every 30 s by default (`SATtypes.h:444`, `SATcontrol.ino:4614`). The PID is a different story: see item 3. (Row 16.)
3. **The firmware integral can build up faster.** Both add one fixed "60 seconds" step per PID update (`pid.py:191`, `SATpid.ino:166`). thermo-nova updates every 60 s (`climate.py:712-716`). The firmware updates on every tick where the room value moved, and about every 60 s when it did not (`SATpid.ino:276-285`). So while the room temperature keeps changing a little, the firmware integral grows up to twice as fast at the 30 s default. With a steady error the rates are the same. (Rows 17 and 18.)
4. **The firmware turns the boiler off at a higher request.** thermo-nova stops heating below a requested 22 °C (`heating_control.py:64-72`). The firmware stops at or below 28.2 °C for radiators (`SATcontrol.ino:130`, `SATcontrol.ino:5206-5214`), as Sergeant D chose (entries 7 and 11) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351). (Row 22.)
5. **On a falling request the firmware can raise the setpoint.** thermo-nova remembers the previous setpoint and never goes above it while the request falls (`heating_control.py:486-489`). The firmware has no memory of it and can jump up to "boiler temperature minus 2 °C" (`SATcontrol.ino:1180-1183`). (Row 23.)
6. **Less is remembered across a restart.** The firmware does not restore the last setpoint, the PWM mode or the PID's last error. It restores the integral only when the saved file is at most 30 minutes old and the clock is set (`SATcontrol.ino:2026-2042`). thermo-nova restores all of these, with no age limit (`heating_control.py:186-187`, `pwm.py:113-115`, `pid.py:147-155`). (Rows 7, 8, 11.)
7. **Without an outside sensor the firmware still heats, as if it were 0 °C outside** (`OTGW-Core.h:82`, `SATcontrol.ino:1302`). thermo-nova sends nothing at all (`climate.py:544-545`). (Row 29.)
8. **A new target takes effect later and does not reset the PID.** thermo-nova resets the PID and acts within 1 s (`climate.py:641-667`). The firmware waits for the next tick and keeps the PID state (`settingStuff.ino:997`). (Row 27.)
9. **Defaults.** The curve coefficient is now 2.0 in both (`const.py:182`, `SATtypes.h:465`; TASK-1347, entry 24) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351); a unit that stores 1.5 keeps it. The radiator maximum is 55 °C in thermo-nova and up to 62 °C in the firmware (`helpers.py:97-99`, `SATcontrol.ino:306`). (Rows 20 and 21.)
10. **thermo-nova sends `PM=25` after every setpoint; the firmware never does** (`coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:197`). The firmware's OT-Direct handler reads it as a request for MsgID 25, the boiler water temperature (`OTDirect.ino:3369-3392`, `OTGW-Core.h:399`). Neither code base says why thermo-nova asks for it. (Row 25.)

### Sources, scope and verdict words

- Ground truth: thermo-nova, the local copy of `Alexwijn/SAT` branch `releases/thermo-nova` at `other-projects/SAT-releases-thermo-nova/custom_components/sat`. The task pins it to commit `7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb`; that pin could not be checked locally (item 1 below).
- Firmware: `src/OTGW-firmware` at commit `51df6b486` (branch `dev`). The verification pass read the working tree at `2c194a329`, one commit later. `git diff --numstat 51df6b486 2c194a329` shows that commit leaves `SATcontrol.ino`, `SATpid.ino`, `SATcycles.ino` and `safeTimers.h` unchanged, and changes the other cited files only by one-for-one line replacements (version banners, a default topic). The cited line numbers therefore hold for both commits.
- Adversarial verification: every row was re-opened on both sides. Each verdict confirmed is marked "(verified)". No verdict was changed and no row was deleted. Rows 2, 6, 8, 11, 14, 25, 28, 33, 37 and 38 kept their verdict but had a statement corrected. Rows 1, 3, 5, 7, 17, 21, 22, 24, 27, 29, 31 and 32 kept their verdict and got added citations or more precise wording.
- Built from `a1-startup-thermonova.md` and `a1-startup-firmware.md`. Every line cited here was re-read in the code for this comparison.
- Verdict words: **match** (same behaviour), **differs** (both have it, but it behaves differently; partial matches are also "differs"), **missing in firmware** (thermo-nova has it, the firmware does not), **firmware-only** (the firmware has it, thermo-nova does not).

### Not found in the code / unclear

1. **thermo-nova pin.** The local copy could not be confirmed as commit `7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb`. It is a plain snapshot inside the firmware repository, without that commit object.
2. **Order of the four "HA started" callbacks** (`climate.py:140-144`). Whether HA runs them in the order they were registered is HA library behaviour. If the PID callback ran before the curve callback, the first PID update would be skipped (`pid.py:161-163`).
3. **NTP timing versus `initSAT()`.** Whether the firmware clock is synced when `initSAT()` runs depends on the network. The code does not wait for it (`OTGW-firmware.ino:486`, `OTGW-firmware.ino:567`). That decides only whether the boot restore of row 11 can succeed; the second restore runs either way.
4. **Meaning of `PM=3`, `PM=15`, `PM=48`, `PM=25` and `MI=500` on the PIC, and why thermo-nova sends them.** Both SAT code bases send them without explaining them (`coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:166-172`, `coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:197`, `SATcontrol.ino:3724-3733`). Only the firmware's OT-Direct command handler states a meaning: `PM=<id>` requests that MsgID on the next cycle (`OTDirect.ino:3369-3392`), and `MI=` sets the minimum gap between OT messages (`OTDirect.ino:3572-3573`). Which boards route commands to that handler was not traced, and the PIC firmware was not read.
5. **`overshoot_protection` default in thermo-nova.** The property reads the config entry data, not the options, and is False when the key is absent (`entry_data.py:149-151`). A grep for `OVERSHOOT_PROTECTION` in `config_flow.py` found no match, so where the key is written was not established. The firmware default is true (`SATtypes.h:465`). This affects the first PWM setpoint and is left to the PWM area.
6. **Whether the firmware can know the manufacturer at `initSAT()`.** That depends on whether OT frames are decoded before `setup()` ends, which was not traced (row 3).
7. **Room and zone PIDs.** thermo-nova runs one PID per room on its own 60 s timer (`area.py:177-190`). The start-up of the firmware zone PIDs (`SATcontrol.ino:4846-4899`) was not traced, so no verdict is given.
8. **First PWM cycle after boot.** How the different flame-off start (row 38) changes the first PWM ON time was not worked out.
9. **What the gateway or boiler does with the last `CS=` and `CH=`** after thermo-nova stops sending (OFF) or during the firmware's start-up gap. That is gateway and boiler behaviour outside both code bases.
10. **Firmware main loop rate.** Ticks fire only when a main-loop pass reaches the timer check (`SATcontrol.ino:4614`). The loop rate is not fixed by the code, so all firmware times above are earliest times.

---

## PID controller

Comparison of the OTGW firmware port (branch `dev`, commit `51df6b486`, paths relative to `src/OTGW-firmware`) against the ground truth, Alexwijn/SAT `releases/thermo-nova` at commit `7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb` (paths relative to `custom_components/sat`). Inputs: `a2-pid-thermonova.md` and `a2-pid-firmware.md`. The lines behind each verdict were re-opened in the source for this comparison: the PID code on both sides (pid.py, SATpid.ino), the control tick, zones, resets, persistence and defaults. A few supporting citations inside a cited range come from the input documents without a separate re-read here: parts of SATcontrol.ino:4428-4486 (auto-tune) and the thermo-nova sensor attribute lines. The thermo-nova input states that it was citation-audited.

Adversarial verification (2026-10-22). Every row with a verdict other than match was re-opened on both sides, and so were the match rows 1, 2, 6, 7, 9, 14, 19, 20 and 21. The working tree was at `2c194a329` during this check. SATpid.ino and SATcontrol.ino did not change between `51df6b486` and `2c194a329`; the other cited firmware files changed only in their version banner, with the same line numbers. " (verified)" after a verdict means that verdict held against the code. Row 42 changed from "missing in firmware" to "differs". Rows 11, 28, 30 to 33, 36, 39, 42, 44 and 46 got corrected text or extra citations.

One correction to the firmware input document. It says there is no separate "freeze integral" setting in the firmware. There is one: `bSolarFreezeIntegral`, default true (SATtypes.h:549). It can be set (settingStuff.ino:1111) and is published (SATcontrol.ino:3204); since TASK-1323 the PID reads it (SATpid.ino:157). See row 18 (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).

Verdicts: **match** means the same behaviour. **differs** means both sides have the feature, but it behaves differently. **missing in firmware** means thermo-nova has it and the firmware does not. **firmware-only** means the firmware has it and thermo-nova does not. "Computed" marks a result worked out from the code, not stated in it. The proposed actions are suggestions. None of them has been decided.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Output structure | `output = heating curve + P + I + D`, in degrees (pid.py:126-132) | `output = heatingCurveValue + P + I + D` (SATpid.ino:308-310) | match (verified) | None | None |
| 2 | Error definition | `round(setpoint - current, 3)`, positive = room too cold (temperature/state.py:15-18) | `roundf((target - room) * 1000) / 1000` (SATpid.ino:63-66) | match (verified) | None | None |
| 3 | Heating curve formula | `4*(T-20) + 0.03*(Tout-20)^2 - 0.4*(Tout-20)`, scaled by coefficient/4, base 20 underfloor or 27.2 other, rounded to 0.1 (heating_curve.py:27-39, types.py:116-118) | Same formula and base offsets, not rounded (SATcontrol.ino:485-500, SATcontrol.ino:49-50) | differs (verified) | Below 0.05 degree (rounding only) | Document as deliberate |
| 4 | Heating curve coefficient default | 2.0 (const.py:182) | 2.0 since TASK-1347 (SATtypes.h:465); a stored value is kept (entry 24) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351) | match (corrected) | None for a fresh unit. A unit that stores 1.5 keeps it and runs a lower flow temperature and weaker P, I and D | Done (TASK-1347, entry 24) |
| 5 | Coefficient setting range | 0.1 to 12, step 0.1 (config_flow.py:616-618) | 0.1 to 5.0 (settingStuff.ino:998); auto-tune limits 0.3 to 5.0 (SATcontrol.ino:4475-4476) | differs (verified) | Coefficients above 5 cannot be set in the firmware | Confirm with Sergeant D |

It is restored after the PID and zone step (SATcontrol.ino:4901-4904), but the summer-simmer and valves-closed returns come before that restore (SATcontrol.ino:4766-4790), and the next tick takes the setting as its new base (SATcontrol.ino:4727). While estimating, the integral stays active over a wider error band and the derivative is held over that band, for the main PID and the zones. Gates: no real update within 10 s, and none within 60 s when the error changed by less than 0.001 (SATpid.ino:21, SATpid.ino:276-285) | differs (verified) | Computed: at the 30 s default, the PID really updates on every tick when the error moves, else every second tick | Align with thermo-nova (60 s) or confirm with Sergeant D |
| 13 | Integral step per update | `ki * error * 60`, with the fixed 60, one update per minute (pid.py:191) | `fKi * error * 60`, fixed 60 (SATpid.ino:166), applied on every real update | differs (verified) | Computed: at the 30 s default with a changing error, the integral grows up to 2 times faster per minute of real time | Align with thermo-nova |

### Most important differences for Sergeant D (plain language)

1. **Rooms/zones combine differently.** With several zones, the firmware does not take "the higher of main and rooms" (climate.py:385-386). One active zone simply replaces the main PID (SATcontrol.ino:4865-4868). With two or more, it always adds 5 degrees on top (SATcontrol.ino:4882), while thermo-nova uses the 5 degrees only as a ceiling (area.py:298-299). The overshoot brake is also much milder (rows 38 to 42).
2. **The boiler-off threshold is higher for radiators.** Thermo-nova heats as soon as the request is above 22 (heating_control.py:72). The firmware switches the boiler off at or below 28.2 for radiators and 21 for underfloor (SATcontrol.ino:130-131, SATcontrol.ino:5206-5214), Sergeant D's values (entries 7 and 11) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).
3. **Changing the target does not reset the PID.** Thermo-nova resets on every target, preset or window change that changes the target value (climate.py:641-660, climate.py:643-644). The firmware does not reset on a target change. The preset "reset" only clears a displayed number, not the controller (SATcontrol.ino:998, SATpid.ino:34).
4. **The integral can build up twice as fast.** The firmware adds a full minute's worth of integral on every real update (SATpid.ino:166). At the default 30 s tick this can happen twice a minute (SATtypes.h:444, SATpid.ino:276-285).
5. **Short boiler-off at start-up.** After a boot, a disable or a flush, the first PID call returns 0 (SATpid.ino:276-280). The firmware then sends `CS=10` and `CH=0` for one or two ticks (SATcontrol.ino:4971-4983). The thermo-nova code shows no such phase: with no stored state its output equals the heating curve value, and with a stored state P, I and D come from the restored values (pid.py:98-99, pid.py:126-132, pid.py:147-155).
6. **The default heating curve matches since TASK-1347.** The coefficient defaults to 2.0 in both (SATtypes.h:465, const.py:182). A unit that already stores 1.5 keeps it (entry 24) and runs a lower flow temperature and weaker P, I and D (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).
7. **The room temperature is smoothed before the PID.** A 45 s filter (SATcontrol.ino:4694-4713) is not in thermo-nova. It delays the reaction to room changes.
8. **The solar "freeze integral" switch now works.** Since TASK-1323 the PID reads it (SATpid.ino:157, SATtypes.h:549) and skips the whole integral update while frozen, as thermo-nova (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351). This only matters when solar gain compensation is turned on, and it is off by default.
9. **ECO mode does nothing.** The setting is stored and published but does not change control (SATcontrol.ino:2533, SATcontrol.ino:2978).
10. **Saved PID memory is used only if it is fresh.** The firmware restores the integral only when the saved file is under 30 minutes old and the clock is set (SATcontrol.ino:2028-2037). Thermo-nova always restores (pid.py:147-155).

What matches: the core PID itself. The error, the heating curve formula, the automatic gain formulas, the "integral only inside the 0.1 band" rule, and the derivative sign, filter and cap are the same (rows 1, 2, 6, 7, 14, 19 to 21).

### Not found in the code / unclear

1. Whether the firmware's start-up "boiler off for one or two ticks" (row 28) was intended. The code does not say. The comment at SATpid.ino:70-71 says the restore is meant to "warm-start", but `fPidOutput` is not restored (SATpid.ino:75-83).
2. Whether the late NTP restore (SATcontrol.ino:4515-4519) can overwrite a live integral in practice. That depends on what `time(nullptr)` returns before NTP sync, which was not checked.
3. `satPidUpdate` received `boilerTemp` but did not use it; the parameter is gone with `satPidUpdate` (TASK-1368). The PID now steps on its own 60 s timer as thermo-nova (`climate.py:712-716`) and the tick reads `satPidOutput()`.
4. Thermo-nova side: whether a wizard choice of manual gains reaches the PID cannot be confirmed from the code (thermo-nova input, unclear item 12). Row 8 therefore compares only the code paths.
5. Thermo-nova side: the `reset_integral` service is registered twice (climate.py:222, services.py:41-46). Both registrations clear the integral and the raw derivative, so row 36 holds either way.
6. Thermo-nova side: area PIDs can be reset repeatedly on climate events outside the HOME preset (climate.py:785-795). The firmware zone code has no equivalent, and the code does not show whether the thermo-nova behaviour is intended, so it is not scored as a row.
7. Row 11: whether the deadband growth in the MQTT-loss fallback that carries over from a summer-simmer or valves-closed tick is intended. The code comment at SATcontrol.ino:4901 says the original value is restored "after PID used the widened value", which suggests it is not.

---

## Heating curve and setpoint selection

Ground truth: Alexwijn/SAT `releases/thermo-nova`, commit 7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb. Thermo-nova paths are relative to `custom_components/sat`. Firmware paths are relative to `src/OTGW-firmware/` at git HEAD 51df6b486 (branch `dev`). Inputs: a3-curve-setpoint-thermonova.md and a3-curve-setpoint-firmware.md. Every row below was checked against the code lines it cites. Impacts marked "derived" are arithmetic on the cited formulas, not statements found in the code.

Adversarial verification (2026-10-22): every row was re-opened on both sides and the verdict tested against other code paths. A verdict marked "(verified)" survived that test. The check ran on working tree 2c194a329. Since 51df6b486 the cited firmware files changed only by same-line replacements (version banners, and `_HOSTNAME` to `MQTT_NAME_DEFAULT` in settingStuff.ino and MQTTstuff.ino), so all cited line numbers are unchanged. The one file whose line count changed, OTGW-firmware.h, is not cited.

Verdicts: **match** (same behaviour), **differs** (both have it, behaviour differs), **missing in firmware** (thermo-nova has it, firmware does not), **firmware-only** (firmware has it, thermo-nova does not).

Rows run in time order: start-up, when things are computed, the curve, the requested setpoint, heater on/off, the continuous rule, what is sent, then telemetry.

| Topic | Thermo-nova (citation) | Firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Start-up: curve value before the first calculation | `None` (heating_curve.py:14). While `None` the requested setpoint is 10 °C (climate.py:372-373) and the PID output is 0.0 (pid.py:129-130) | 0.0 (SATtypes.h:154). No `None` state | differs (verified) | Low. Thermo-nova computes the curve at once at start-up (next row), so the `None` window is short. Before its first tick the firmware's only setpoint command is `CS=0` (SATcontrol.ino:3706-3710) | None |
| Start-up: first curve and setpoint | Computed immediately when HA is running, or at the "HA started" event: curve, listeners, PID, control loop (climate.py:133-144) | First control tick one `iControlInterval` after `initSAT()` restarts the timer, default 30 s (SATcontrol.ino:3719, safeTimers.h:131-135, SATcontrol.ino:4614, SATtypes.h:444). Until then `CS=0` hands control to the thermostat (SATcontrol.ino:3706-3710) | differs (verified) | Derived: the firmware starts heating control up to 30 s later. During that time the thermostat keeps control | None, or document |
| Start-up: room target restore | Restored from the saved HA attribute `temperature`, else `min_temp` (climate.py:176-182) | Persisted setting `SATtargettemp`, read at boot (settingStuff.ino:319, settingStuff.ino:997) | match (verified) | None: both keep the last target across a restart | None |
| Start-up: PID state restore | Always loaded from storage (pid.py:141-157, climate.py:131) | Loaded only when NTP time is set and the file is at most 1800 s old (SATcontrol.ino:1993, SATcontrol.ino:2027-2037). If NTP is not set at init, the load is retried once at the first control pass after NTP sync (SATcontrol.ino:4512-4519). A disable or safety trip resets the PID and saves zeros (SATcontrol.ino:2170-2171) | differs (verified) | After an outage longer than 30 min the firmware starts with integral 0; thermo-nova keeps the old integral. A firmware disable or safety trip also makes the next start cold, even within 30 min | Sergeant D to confirm whether the age limit is wanted |

Sent only in `initSAT()` when SAT is enabled at boot and an OT command interface exists (SATcontrol.ino:3721-3725); a grep found no other sender | differs (verified) | Same commands. BLE, multi-area and external MQTT values return before this step (SATcontrol.ino:1208-1231) | differs (verified) | Only with thermal comfort on (default off in both: const.py:131, SATtypes.h:522). Two effects: a non-OpenTherm room sensor gets no index in the firmware, and losing humidity stops the thermo-nova PID but not the firmware | Sergeant D to confirm |
| Input: room temperature smoothing | No smoothing of the room temperature.
