Left out of the table: thermo-nova's switch backend, where the requested setpoint ceiling becomes the configured minimum setpoint (coordinator/switch/__init__.py:41-43). The firmware has no switch backend.

### Most important differences for Sergeant D

1. **No outside sensor.** Thermo-nova stops and sends nothing (climate.py:544-545). The firmware assumes 0 °C outside and keeps heating (OTGW-Core.h:82, SATcontrol.ino:1302).
2. **No room sensor.** It is the other way round. Thermo-nova keeps heating on the curve plus the last P term (climate.py:468-482, climate.py:544-545, pid.py:98-101). The firmware stops after 10 bad ticks, hands the boiler back to the thermostat with `CS=0` (SATcontrol.ino:4669-4683, SATcontrol.ino:2179), and stays off until SAT is enabled again or the device reboots (SATcontrol.ino:4580-4581, SATcontrol.ino:1936-1948).
3. **When the heater switches off.** Thermo-nova: requested flow 22 °C or lower, for every system (heating_control.py:72). Firmware: at or below 28.2 °C for radiators and 21 °C for underfloor (SATchEnable.h:26-29, SATcontrol.ino:5209, SATcontrol.ino:130-131), Sergeant D's values (entries 7 and 11). Derived, radiators at target 20 °C with coefficient 2.0 (the firmware default since TASK-1347): the firmware stops heating above about 16 °C outside; thermo-nova never stops on the curve alone. (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).
4. **Several rooms.** Thermo-nova uses the 5 °C headroom as a ceiling, and for up to 4 rooms always requests the highest room output (area.py:294-301). Its `max` never lets the rooms output lower the main demand (climate.py:385-386). The firmware adds the 5 °C and lets one zone replace the main demand (SATcontrol.ino:4882, SATcontrol.ino:4867). Example (derived): rooms at 40 and 45 °C give 45 °C in thermo-nova and 50 °C in the firmware.
5. **A room that is too warm.** Thermo-nova caps the flow at `22 + 10 - 4 x degrees over`, so 2.5 °C too warm switches the heater off (area.py:322-331). The firmware only subtracts the degrees over (SATcontrol.ino:4887).
6. **Falling demand with a hot boiler.** Thermo-nova holds the previous setpoint (heating_control.py:488-489). The firmware can raise it, for example from 45 to 48 °C (derived) (SATcontrol.ino:1182-1186).
7. **Solar gain.** Thermo-nova lowers the demand, which can switch the heater off (climate.py:391-392). The firmware now does the same: it lowers the requested setpoint before the cold cutoff, so solar can switch the heater off (SATcontrol.ino:5137-5152, SATcontrol.ino:5209; TASK-1323, entry 11). (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).
8. **Defaults.** Curve coefficient now 2.0 in both (SATtypes.h:465, TASK-1347, entry 24; corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351; before that 1.5, about 2.5 °C cooler at 0 °C outside, derived), radiator maximum 55 °C against 62 °C, control loop every 5 s against 30 s (const.py:182, helpers.py:99, SATcontrol.ino:306, climate.py:706-710, SATtypes.h:467 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]).
9. **ECO mode** does nothing in the firmware (climate.py:377-379 has no counterpart; SATtypes.h:524 is never read in control).

### Not found in the code / unclear

1. Why the firmware uses 28.2 °C and 21 °C as cold setpoints instead of 22 °C. Only a code comment naming a person and task exists (SATcontrol.ino:262-263). Not checked further.
2. Why the firmware radiator cap is 62 °C. Only a code comment gives a reason (SATcontrol.ino:216-227). Not checked against any standard.
3. What `PM=25` after every `CS=` does in thermo-nova (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:197). The code does not say, so the impact of leaving it out in the firmware is unknown.
4. Whether the thermo-nova room aggregate is meant as a ceiling (`min(highest, baseline + 5)`, area.py:299) or the firmware reading (`p75 + 5`) was an intended change. No firmware comment states it was deliberate (SATcontrol.ino:4880-4882).
5. Whether thermo-nova's "leave the last command in place" on HVAC off (climate.py:544-545) is intended. The firmware sends `CS=0` instead (SATcontrol.ino:2179).
6. Whether thermo-nova's lack of a DHW check in the continuous path (heating_control.py:450-508) matters in practice. Not tested here.
7. How a coefficient change reaches a running thermo-nova curve, since it is stored once (heating_curve.py:13-16). Not checked in this comparison.
8. The Immergas register 11:12 meaning. Thermo-nova writes a modulation value (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:218); its meaning on the boiler is not documented in either code base.
9. Thermo-nova `Area.async_outside_entity_changed` would update a room curve with the room temperature in the place of the outside temperature (area.py:198-200). A grep found no caller, so it is dead code and not behaviour.
10. Whether the firmware's 2 h thermal safe fallback can ever start. `iLastKnownRoomMs` is refreshed whenever `satGetRoomTemp()` returns an in-range value (SATcontrol.ino:4105-4114), and in fallback mode that function can return the estimate itself (SATcontrol.ino:1249-1253). Possible, not tested.
11. Whether the auto-detected manufacturer is already known when `initSAT()` decides on `MI=500` (SATcontrol.ino:3730, SATcontrol.ino:164-170, SATcontrol.ino:175-180). Not checked.

---

## PWM, modulation and the suppression mechanism

Abbreviations: TN = thermo-nova (ground truth), FW = OTGW firmware. CS = control setpoint, MM = maximum relative modulation, CH = central-heating enable. "Requested setpoint" is `requested_setpoint` in TN and `pidOutput` in FW. TN paths are relative to `custom_components/sat`; FW paths are relative to `src/OTGW-firmware`.

"(verified)" after a verdict means both cited sides were reopened and checked against the code in the adversarial verification pass (see "Sources and method").

| Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Suppression: what it writes | Only CS; suppression does not write MM (heating_control.py:439-448) | Only CS (SATcontrol.ino:709-717, SATcontrol.ino:884-888, SATcontrol.ino:907-917) | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| MM for a gas boiler while PWM is on | MM = 0 whenever PWM is enabled and DHW is off, in the ON, OFF and IDLE status alike (heating_control.py:136-140, heating_control.py:517-518, const.py:37) | MM = 0 in PWM mode, ON and OFF phase, when the source is not a heat pump or hybrid (SATcontrol.ino:304-314, SATcontrol.ino:4962) | match (verified) | None for a gas boiler. | None. |
| CS in the PWM OFF phase | CS = 10 °C (heating_control.py:388-390, const.py:35) | CS = 10 °C (SATcontrol.ino:875-878, SATcontrol.ino:54) | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| Flame-off hold: formula and default | CS = return + `flame_off_setpoint_offset_celsius`, default 18.0 °C, range 0 to 30 (heating_control.py:397-401, const.py:151, config_flow.py:764-767) | CS = return + `fFlameOffOffset`, default 18.0 °C, range 0 to 30 (SATcontrol.ino:701-707, SATtypes.h:462, settingStuff.ino:1034) | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| Flame-off hold: clamp | Clamped to [22 °C, `limits.maximum_setpoint`] (heating_control.py:398, const.py:34); default maximum 55 °C, 50 °C underfloor (helpers.py:97-99, entry_data.py:229-235) | Clamped to [cold setpoint 28.2 °C radiators or 21 °C underfloor, system cap 62 / 45 / 40 °C] (SATcontrol.ino:704-705, SATcontrol.ino:879, SATcontrol.ino:55-56, SATcontrol.ino:228-235), then to the effective maximum (SATcontrol.ino:4937) | differs (verified) | Only at the edges. With return above 37 °C, TN caps the hold at 55 °C and FW at 62 °C (or the effective maximum). With return below 10.2 °C, FW raises the hold to 28.2 °C. | Sergeant D decides which bounds are intended. If FW keeps its own, record it in an ADR. |
| Hold after ignition | While less than `modulation_suppression_delay_seconds` has passed since flame-on, CS = the stored hold (heating_control.py:414-426) | Same: the stored hold while `fModSupDelay` has not passed (SATcontrol.ino:884-885) | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| Suppression delay: default, range, reference point | 20 s, range 0 to 120, counted from the device tracker's last flame OFF-to-ON transition, DHW ignitions included (const.py:152, config_flow.py:768-771, heating_control.py:410-417, device/__init__.py:87-88, device/__init__.py:209-211) | 20 s, range 0 to 120, counted from the cycle tracker's last real flame-on edge, DHW ignitions included; edges are checked on every loop pass (SATtypes.h:455, settingStuff.ino:1014, SATcontrol.ino:720-723, SATcycles.ino:701-714, SATcontrol.ino:4592-4596) | match (verified) | None. Both count from the moment the flame was seen to light. | None. |
| Suppression setpoint: formula and default | CS = flow - `modulation_suppression_offset_celsius`, default 1.0 °C (heating_control.py:440-441, const.py:153) | CS = flow - `fModSupOffset`, default 1.0 °C (SATcontrol.ino:713, SATtypes.h:456) | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| Suppression offset: allowed range | 0 to 10 °C (config_flow.py:772-775) | 0 to 5 °C (settingStuff.ino:1015, restAPI.ino:4074) | differs (verified) | None at the default. An offset above 5 °C cannot be set in FW. | Widen the FW range to 0 to 10, or record why 5. |
| Suppressed setpoint: clamp | No clamp, neither in the control code nor in the coordinator that sends it (heating_control.py:441-448, coordinator/__init__.py:357-360, coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:195-199) | Clamped to [10 °C, system cap], then to the effective maximum (SATcontrol.ino:714-715, SATcontrol.ino:909, SATcontrol.ino:4936-4937) | differs (verified) | Only when the flow is below 11 °C or above the cap. No effect at normal flow temperatures. | Record as a deliberate safety clamp. |
| How often the suppressed CS is recomputed | On every control tick, about every 5 s (heating_control.py:441-448, climate.py:706-710) | On every 30 s tick, plus a separate 3 s refresh that sends only CS (SATcontrol.ino:887-888, SATcontrol.ino:894-918, SATcontrol.ino:480) | differs (verified) | CS follows the flow at a similar rate (3 s against about 5 s). FW sends a CS only when the value changed by 0.1 °C or 300 s passed (SATcontrol.ino:383-396); on PIC boards it also re-sends the last CS every 30 s (SATcontrol.ino:403-414, SATcontrol.ino:4608). | None needed. Document the refresh as a firmware addition. |
| How suppression ends | No timer. It is replaced by CS = 10 °C when the phase goes OFF, by the hold when the flame goes off, and by the continuous rule when PWM stops (heating_control.py:388-408, heating_control.py:147-152, heating_control.py:224-228) | No timer. CS = 10 °C at ON expiry, the hold on flame-off, the continuous rule after leaving PWM (SATcontrol.ino:816-821, SATcontrol.ino:875-883, SATcontrol.ino:4929-4933) | match (verified) | None in the logic. The FW tick delay is covered in the "Control tick interval" row. | None. |
| When the stored hold is cleared | Only when suppression applies. PWM OFF, disable and reset keep it (heating_control.py:439, heating_control.py:388-390, pwm.py:87-109; a search for `_flame_off_hold_setpoint` finds writes only at heating_control.py:95, 401 and 439) | At ON expiry, at the ignition timeout, on every OFF-phase pass, in the OFF branch, at suppression and at reset (SATcontrol.ino:819, SATcontrol.ino:831, SATcontrol.ino:835, SATcontrol.ino:876, SATcontrol.ino:887, SATcontrol.ino:929) | differs (verified) | In TN, a hold from an earlier ON phase can be reused when a new ON phase starts with the flame already on. FW uses the previous CS instead. Narrow case. | Sergeant D decides whether the TN reuse is intended. No FW change until then. |
| Missing return or flow temperature in the override | Return unknown with the flame off, or flow unknown after the delay: CS unchanged and a warning is logged (heating_control.py:392-395, heating_control.py:435-437) | No validity check on the return: the stored `Tret` is used as is in the flame-off hold (SATcontrol.ino:808-814). Both values start at 0.0 at boot (OTGW-Core.h:80, OTGW-Core.h:83) and are written only when MsgID 25 or 28 arrives (OTGW-Core.ino:4512, OTGW-Core.ino:4515). Flow: the control tick treats a flow of 0 or less as invalid, in PWM too: `satApplyPWM` falls back to the continuous setpoint (SATcontrol.ino:1020-1022), and `satApplyContinuous` returns the requested setpoint (SATcontrol.ino:1364-1369). The 3 s suppression refresh has no flow check (SATcontrol.ino:1045-1065) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351): was "the PWM paths do not" | differs (verified) | With a missing sensor, TN keeps the old CS. FW computes CS from the stored value. Arithmetic from the code: if the return was never reported, the hold is 0 + 18 = 18 °C, raised to the 28.2 °C cold setpoint (SATcontrol.ino:810-811). If the flow was never reported, the control tick sends the requested setpoint, while the 3 s refresh (flame on, hold elapsed) sends flow minus the offset, -1 °C raised to 10 °C (SATcontrol.ino:818-824); the two can alternate (derived from the code, not run). CH stays 1: without a flow temperature PWM does not drive the CS (`satPwmDrivesSetpoint`, SATcontrol.ino:1010-1014), so `satChEnableDecide` gives 1 while the requested setpoint is above the cold setpoint (SATchEnable.h:46-52, SATcontrol.ino:5223) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351): was "the control tick then sends CH = 0", and before that "in the PWM ON phase `satChEnableDecide` keeps CH on". | Add a validity check that keeps the previous CS, as TN does. |
| Fallback CS when the override does not apply | CS unchanged: the previous tick's value stays (heating_control.py:385-386, heating_control.py:428-433) | `prevCS` = the last control CS if above 10 °C, else the requested setpoint (SATcontrol.ino:870, SATcontrol.ino:873, SATcontrol.ino:885, SATcontrol.ino:4939) | differs (verified) | Same result unless the previous CS was 10 °C. Then TN keeps 10 °C (CH off) and FW sends the requested setpoint (CH on). | Sergeant D decides. The FW comment states this choice (SATcontrol.ino:858-862). |
| Default of overshoot protection (the switch for the whole CS sequence) | Read from the entry data; False when absent (entry_data.py:149-151). `OPTIONS_DEFAULTS` holds False for it (const.py:122), but the property reads the entry data, not the options (__init__.py:29). No configuration step sets it; only a migration from old options does (__init__.py:159-162; a search for "overshoot_protection" in all .py files finds no other writer) | `bOvershootProtection` default true (SATtypes.h:488) | differs (verified) | Large. On a new TN install the flame-off hold and suppression never run: in PWM, CS stays at its previous value and only MM = 0 acts (heating_control.py:385-386). FW runs the full CS sequence by default. | Sergeant D decides the intended default. If FW keeps true, record it in an ADR. |
| PWM with overshoot protection off | CS frozen at the previous value; the PWM state machine still runs; MM = 0 (heating_control.py:385-386, heating_control.py:211-215, heating_control.py:517-518) | CS = `prevCS` on every tick; the schedule still runs; MM = 0 (SATcontrol.ino:870-874, SATcontrol.ino:308) | match (verified) | None beyond the "Fallback CS" row. | None. |
| CS during DHW in PWM | CS unchanged (heating_control.py:385-386) | The tick returns before any command and the refresh stops on the DHW bit, so the last CS stays (SATcontrol.ino:4865-4882 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351], SATcontrol.ino:901) | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| MM during DHW in PWM | MM = `maximum_relative_modulation`, because the modulation state is HOT_WATER (heating_control.py:136-137, heating_control.py:517-520) | No MM is sent; the boiler keeps the last MM, which is 0 in PWM. The only MM callers are at SATcontrol.ino:4741, SATcontrol.ino:4771, SATcontrol.ino:4784 and SATcontrol.ino:4981 (SATcontrol.ino:4865-4882 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351], SATcontrol.ino:416-427) | differs (verified) | TN lifts the modulation limit for hot water. FW leaves MM = 0 on the boiler during a DHW draw in PWM. What the boiler does with that is not in either code. | Send MM = `iMaxRelModulation` when DHW starts, as TN does. High priority. |
| PWM state machine during DHW | Keeps running on every tick (heating_control.py:202-215, pwm.py:117-217) | Frozen: the tick returns before `satApplyPWM()` (SATcontrol.ino:4865-4882 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351], SATcontrol.ino:4929-4930) | differs (verified) | After DHW, the TN phase timing has moved on; FW resumes the same phase. FW also counts the DHW ignition as a flame-on edge, which moves the ON-phase reference and the hourly count (SATcontrol.ino:4592-4596, SATcycles.ino:714, SATcycles.ino:722-724). | Sergeant D decides. Low priority. |
| 300 s guard after DHW | Blocks the overshoot enable, and resets the underheat and saturation timers, during DHW and for 300 s after it (heating_control.py:268-274, heating_control.py:313-315, const.py:19) | Since TASK-1420 (entry 108): the DHW early return calls `satCycleNoteDhwTick(_sat_tickMs)` (SATcontrol.ino:5241, SATcycles.ino:200-205), which stores the DHW end on the tick clock and clears the sustained starts. `satDhwGuardActive()` (SATcycles.ino:164-167) blocks the overshoot switch (SATcycles.ino:1043) and resets the PWM exits (SATcycles.ino:1072) during the draw and for 300 s after it. The boot value keeps the guard inactive (SATcycles.ino:146), except in the 300 s before each tick-clock wrap, about every 49.7 days (arithmetic) | match (host test; not checked on hardware) | None. | None. |
| Heat-demand threshold (cold setpoint) | 22 °C for every heating system (const.py:34, heating_control.py:64-72) | 28.2 °C radiators, 21 °C underfloor (SATcontrol.ino:130-131, SATcontrol.ino:339-343) | differs (verified) | Radiators with a requested setpoint between 22 and 28.2 °C: TN heats, FW sends CS = 10 °C and CH = 0 (SATcontrol.ino:5206-5214, SATcontrol.ino:5223). | Decided by Sergeant D: 28.2 / 21 everywhere (entries 7 and 11, answer 2). Recorded in ADR-195 (Accepted). Its statement that the `<=` boundary is settled by entry 12 is wrong (entries 7 and 11 keep it open); ADR-199 (Proposed, TASK-1355) supersedes it (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351). |
| No demand while PWM is on | Valves closed, or requested setpoint at or below 22 °C: PWM disabled and reset, sustain timers reset, CS = 10 °C, MM = maximum (heating_control.py:229-234) | Valves closed, or requested setpoint at or below the cold setpoint: CS = 10 °C, MM = `iMaxRelModulation`, CH = 0, and `satPwmDisableOnHeaterOff()` leaves PWM, resets the PWM state and the sustain guards and sets the previous CS to 10 (SATcontrol.ino:1100-1109, SATcontrol.ino:5008-5021, SATcontrol.ino:5206-5214). In the cold cutoff `satApplyPWM()` still runs its schedule on that tick before the reset (SATcontrol.ino:5168, SATcontrol.ino:5209-5213). Summer simmer (firmware-only) sends CS = 10, MM = maximum, CH = 0 but leaves the mode and the PWM state as they are (SATcontrol.ino:4994-5006) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351): was "the mode stays PWM and nothing is reset"; TASK-1327 | match except summer simmer (corrected) | After valves closed or a cold cutoff both start in continuous mode. After summer simmer the firmware resumes PWM with the phase state it kept. | Done for valves closed and the cold cutoff (entry 12). Summer simmer has no thermo-nova counterpart; entry 32 keeps the feature but does not say what it does to PWM. |
| PWM disable when the request reaches the maximum | `requested_setpoint >= limits.maximum_setpoint`, checked on every tick with demand (heating_control.py:203-204). ### The most important differences, in plain language

What already matches, with overshoot protection on in both (this is the part that earlier AI work got wrong, so it is worth confirming; see item 1 below for the thermo-nova default):

- Suppression writes only the control setpoint, never a modulation value (heating_control.py:439-448; SATcontrol.ino:709-717).
- During PWM the boiler gets MM = 0 the whole time (heating_control.py:517-518; SATcontrol.ino:308).
- In the OFF phase CS = 10 °C (heating_control.py:388-390; SATcontrol.ino:875-878).
- While waiting for the flame, CS = return + 18 °C (heating_control.py:397-401; SATcontrol.ino:701-707).
- For 20 s after the flame lights, that value is held (heating_control.py:414-426; SATcontrol.ino:884-885).
- After the 20 s, CS = flow - 1 °C (heating_control.py:440-441; SATcontrol.ino:713).

Where the firmware differs, most important first:

1. **Whether the CS sequence runs at all.** In thermo-nova "overshoot protection" is off unless an old setting was migrated, and no settings screen sets it (entry_data.py:149-151, __init__.py:159-162). With it off, PWM only sends MM = 0 and leaves CS alone (heating_control.py:385-386). The firmware has it on by default (SATtypes.h:488). Which one was intended?
2. **How long the burner runs per cycle (changed by TASK-1273, impact now low).** Thermo-nova computes the duty cycle from the real boiler flow temperature (pwm.py:232). The firmware now does the same: BT is seeded with the flow at the first flame, at the first ignition of an ON phase and at an ON start with a burning flame, filtered during the 180 s after that and floored at base + 1 (SATcontrol.ino:749-751, SATcontrol.ino:764-782, SATcontrol.ino:875-881). The fixed 47.2 °C is gone, and without a flow temperature PWM is idle as in thermo-nova (SATcontrol.ino:741-748, SATcontrol.ino:905-907). Requested 40 °C with the boiler at 60 °C gives 39 % burn time in both (arithmetic). What remains is in the row "Learning the effective boiler temperature": no reseed for a range-5 stint that starts with a lit flame, and fewer filter samples at the 30 s tick.
3. **The phase clock.** Thermo-nova times ON and OFF from when the phase starts (pwm.py:162, pwm.py:208). The firmware times them from the last real flame-on and flame-off (SATcontrol.ino:834, SATcontrol.ino:859). It also ends the ON phase after 180 s without ignition, where thermo-nova carries on (SATcontrol.ino:844-855; pwm.py:197-199). And the firmware can start an ON phase at very low duty, where thermo-nova keeps the burner off (pwm.py:155).
4. **Hot water during PWM.** Thermo-nova raises MM to the maximum during DHW (heating_control.py:136-137). The firmware sends nothing, so MM = 0 stays on the boiler (SATcontrol.ino:4865-4882 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]). Since TASK-1420 the 300 s "after DHW" guard is refreshed on every DHW tick (SATcycles.ino:200-205).
5. **Entering PWM sooner.** The firmware switches to PWM at 2 °C overshoot instead of 3 °C (SATtypes.h:454; cycles/const.py:10). Since TASK-1367 and TASK-1420 (entries 41, 86) its timer counts from the first qualifying tick, as thermo-nova, and also works with a tick above 60 s (SATcycles.ino:1043-1062); it fires on the first tick at least 60 s after the first qualifying tick, never earlier (ticks are never closer than one interval, entry 109).
6. **Switching driven by finished cycles is missing.** Thermo-nova also enters PWM after a continuous cycle classified as overshoot, and leaves it after a PWM cycle classified as underheat (pwm.py:219-227). The firmware has neither.
7. **When the boiler counts as "no heat needed".** For radiators the firmware uses 28.2 °C, thermo-nova 22 °C (SATcontrol.ino:130-131, SATcontrol.ino:339-343; const.py:34). CH follows the requested setpoint and the PWM phase in the firmware (`satChEnableDecide`, SATchEnable.h:46-52), the sent CS above 22 °C in thermo-nova (heating_control.py:238). With no demand both now leave PWM (heating_control.py:229-234; SATcontrol.ino:1100-1109), except on the firmware-only summer simmer path. Entries 7, 9, 11, 12 and 17 settled these. (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).
8. **Speed.** Thermo-nova decides every 5 s, the firmware every 30 s (climate.py:706-710; SATtypes.h:444). Only the suppression value is refreshed every 3 s (SATcontrol.ino:480).
9. **Smaller differences.** Immergas gets the setpoint instead of the modulation limit in `TP=11:12` (SATcontrol.ino:4988; coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:218). Leaving PWM at full demand compares with the wrong maximum (SATcontrol.ino:4917). The default is 3 cycles per hour, not 4 (SATtypes.h:525; const.py:119). The hourly cycle cap works very differently (SATcycles.ino:286-292; pwm.py:156). A missing return or flow temperature is not detected in PWM (SATcontrol.ino:703, SATcontrol.ino:713; heating_control.py:392-395).

### Firmware comments that do not match the code

These are noted because comments like these are how wrong descriptions of SAT spread.

- SATtypes.h:456 says the suppression offset is "°C below setpoint"; the code subtracts it from the flow temperature (SATcontrol.ino:713).
- SATcontrol.ino:822-823 used to say the ignition timeout matches pwm.py:194-202 while the code aborted the ON phase; fixed by TASK-1382 (the abort is gone, the 180 s wait now ends and the ON timer runs, as pwm.py:197-199).
- SATcontrol.ino mentions a "3.0C overshoot margin"; the default margin is 2.0 °C (SATtypes.h:454). The 300 s DHW post-overshoot guard is refreshed on DHW ticks since TASK-1420 (SATcycles.ino:200-205).
- SATcontrol.ino:59 defines `SAT_PWM_MIN_ON_SEC` "matches Python"; the constant is not used (a search for the name finds only this definition). The 180 s comes from `satGetMinOnTimeSec()` (SATcontrol.ino:284-290).

### Not found in the code / unclear

1. **PWM entered during a long continuous burn (firmware).** Reading SATcontrol.ino:813-821 and SATcontrol.ino:836-853 suggests the ON phase can end at the next tick and CS can drop to 10 °C. This was derived from the code; no scenario was run. The thermo-nova outcome in the same case (ON phase timed from entry, pwm.py:162-164) was also derived only.
2. **Resolved by TASK-1273: whether the firmware filter takes a sample at the default 30 s tick.** The window is now 180 000 ms after the ignition anchor (SATcontrol.ino:696, SATcontrol.ino:768), so about five ticks fall inside it (derived from the interval, SATtypes.h:444; not run). The control timer catches up missed ticks by adding the interval to the previous due time (SATcontrol.ino:476; safeTimers.h:192-194), so the tick at exactly 180 s is not counted; whether jitter moves it inside the window was not traced. With a control interval of 180 s or more no sample lands.
3. **What the boiler does with CS = flow - 1 °C and MM = 0**, or with MM = 0 during DHW. This is boiler behaviour and is in neither code.
4. **The meaning of Immergas TSP 11/12.** Neither code says what the boiler does with it.
5. **Whether three thermo-nova details are intended:** the cycle cap compared with 780 (pwm.py:156, entry_data.py:220), overshoot protection not settable in the configuration flow (entry_data.py:151), and the force-PWM option never read (entry_data.py:223). The code shows what happens, not the intent.
6. **Thermo-nova range 5 at the end of the on-time.** With off-time 0, an ON phase that ends would pass through OFF (CS = 10 °C) for one tick before the next ON (pwm.py:154-155, pwm.py:205-215, heating_control.py:388-390). This is derived; the 300 s saturation rule normally disables PWM first (heating_control.py:358-378).

7. **Whether the thermo-nova options screen can ever show its heat-pump cycle dropdown.** The branch tests the heating system in the options (config_flow.py:686), the options default it to radiators (config_flow.py:808-812, const.py:184), and the flow stores the user's choice in the entry data (config_flow.py:412-413). Searches for "heating_system" in config_flow.py and __init__.py found no writer into the options; older migrations only read it from there (__init__.py:121-138). So for a new install the radiator dropdown (3 or 4) appears whatever the system is. This is derived; the options screen was not run.

Resolved during verification and moved into the table: the boot values of `Tret` and `Tboiler` (row "Missing return or flow temperature in the override") and how the thermo-nova device tracker records the flame-on time (row "Suppression delay: default, range, reference point").

### Sources and method

- Thermo-nova: Alexwijn/SAT `releases/thermo-nova` at commit 7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb. Local copy: `other-projects/SAT-releases-thermo-nova/custom_components/sat`.
- Firmware: branch `dev`. The code was read at 2c194a329. Between 51df6b486 and 2c194a329, SATcontrol.ino, SATcycles.ino and safeTimers.h are unchanged; SATtypes.h, OTGW-Core.h and OTGW-Core.ino changed only their version banner (line 4); settingStuff.ino changed its banner and one line each at 574 and 808 (MQTT topic default), with no change in line count. So the line numbers above also hold for 51df6b486.

The heat-pump row was briefly considered for "differs" because of a heat-pump dropdown in the thermo-nova options screen; that dropdown only affects configuration and appears to be unreachable for new installs (unclear item 7), so the row stays "firmware-only". ## Boiler state, flame and cycle tracking

Comparison of thermo-nova (ground truth: Alexwijn/SAT `releases/thermo-nova`, commit 7beaaf531, paths relative to `custom_components/sat`) with the OTGW firmware (branch `dev`, HEAD 51df6b486, paths relative to `src/OTGW-firmware/` unless they start with `src/libraries/`).

Where the citations come from: every citation is taken from the two area documents (a5-cycles-device-thermonova.md and a5-cycles-device-firmware.md). Rows marked **(re-read)** were also checked against the code for this comparison. Statements marked "derived from the order of the code" were read, not run on a device or in a test.

Adversarial verification pass (2026-10-22): every row below was re-opened in both code bases and an attempt was made to refute its verdict. "(verified)" after a verdict means both cited locations were opened and the verdict held. "(corrected)" means the verdict was changed in this pass. Citations and impact texts were fixed in place where the code said something different. No row was deleted. Firmware lines were read in the working tree at 2c194a329. SATcontrol.ino and SATcycles.ino are unchanged since 51df6b486; the other cited files show one changed line each (`git diff --stat 51df6b486 2c194a329`), which for SATtypes.h, OTGW-Core.ino, restAPI.ino and OTDirect.ino is the version banner (`git diff`). So the line numbers are the same as at 51df6b486.

Verdicts: **match** (same behaviour), **differs** (both have it, behaviour differs), **missing in firmware** (thermo-nova has it, firmware does not), **firmware-only** (firmware has it, thermo-nova does not). Nuance (defect, label only, deliberate) is in the impact column.

"Label only" means the item changes a status text, an MQTT/REST value or a statistic, and no code path from it to a boiler command was found in either code base.

| Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. What a cycle is | One flame-on period, from flame OFF->ON to ON->OFF (cycles/tracker.py:57-84) | Same edges: OFF->ON opens, ON->OFF closes (SATcycles.ino:701, SATcycles.ino:757) | match (verified) | None | None |
| 2. Flame signal **(re-read)** | OTGW MQTT topic `flame` == "ON" (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:13-15, coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:63-65). This gateway publishes `flame` from slave status bit 3 (OTGW-Core.ino:2442) | `Statusflags & 0x08` (SATcontrol.ino:1337-1341). `Statusflags` is filled by `print_status` (OTGW-Core.ino:4548) as master status in the high byte and slave status in the low byte (OTGW-Core.ino:2870), so bit 3 is the slave flame bit. Under simulation the simulated flame is used instead (SATcontrol.ino:1339) | match (verified) | None: same OpenTherm bit | None |
| 3. Hot water signal **(re-read)** | One signal per backend: `domestichotwater` == "ON" (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:22, coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:67-69); this gateway publishes it from slave status bit 2 (OTGW-Core.ino:2441) | Two sources: `bDhwActive` = `SlaveStatus & 0x04`, refreshed on the control tick (SATcontrol.ino:4855-4859 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]), and `Statusflags` bit 2 read per 1 Hz sample for the cycle kind and the DHW-in-tail test on the real bus; under SAT simulation that counter uses `bDhwActive` (SATcycles.ino:907-929) [lines refreshed 2026-10-25, TASK-1387 review]. The low byte of `Statusflags` is `SlaveStatus` (OTGW-Core.ino:2870) | differs (verified) | Same bit on a real bus; a simulated draw now reaches the cycle kind too (TASK-1387 review). ### Most important differences, for Sergeant D

Ranked by whether they change what goes to the boiler today.

1. **One bad cycle does not switch the control mode in the firmware.** In thermo-nova, an overshooting cycle in continuous mode turns PWM on, and an underheating PWM cycle turns PWM off (pwm.py:219-227, heating_control.py:167). The firmware has no such step. It only switches after the flow has stayed more than 2 °C above the requested setpoint for 60 s in a row, or after 180 s of underheat, or after 300 s of PWM saturation (SATcycles.ino:969-1020). So a cycle that thermo-nova would judge as overshoot, without such a 60 s stretch, keeps the firmware in continuous mode. This follows from reading the code; it was not observed on a boiler. (Row 40.)
2. **A PWM burn can be cut off before the flame lights.** Thermo-nova waits up to 180 s for ignition and then starts its timer at the ignition (pwm.py:186-199). The firmware times the burn from the last flame-on it saw (SATcontrol.ino:813-820). If the flame has not lit by the next PWM check, that is the previous burn, so the new burn ends at once. This was read in the code, not seen on a boiler. (Row 44.)
3. **The 300 s pause after hot water works in the firmware since TASK-1420.** Thermo-nova blocks the overshoot switch to PWM for 300 s after a hot water draw (heating_control.py:272) and resets the PWM exits in the same window (:313-315). The firmware stores the DHW end on every DHW tick (SATcycles.ino:200-205, SATcontrol.ino:5241). Before that it never armed. (Row 42.)
4. **The firmware switches at different temperatures.** It switches to PWM at 2 °C overshoot instead of 3 °C (SATtypes.h:454, heating_control.py:288). With radiators it does not auto-switch at all below a requested 28.2 °C instead of 22 °C (SATcontrol.ino:130-131, SATcontrol.ino:5542-5546, const.py:34) [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]. Code comments suggest both were chosen on purpose (SATtypes.h:454, SATcontrol.ino:337). (Rows 34, 41.)
5. **The firmware caps PWM starts per hour; thermo-nova, as coded, does not.** With default settings the firmware holds the next PWM burn after 3 flame-ons in the last hour, hot water ignitions included (SATtypes.h:525, SATcontrol.ino:271-282, SATcontrol.ino:836-854, SATcycles.ino:723). Thermo-nova compares its start count with the duty-cycle setting in seconds (780 by default), which an hour of PWM cannot reach (pwm.py:156, entry_data.py:220, const.py:167; derived). (Row 45.)
6. **The firmware's cycle judgement has defects, but today they change labels, not heating.** A sorting bug gives a wrong p90 for cycles longer than about 130 s and writes outside its memory (SATcycles.ino:525-533). Off-time is always 0, so the 4 h and 24 h duty ratios are always 1.0 and the published average duty ratio climbs toward 1.0 (SATcycles.ino:760, SATcycles.ino:816-820, SATcycles.ino:594-599). Hot water cycles are judged like heating cycles (SATcycles.ino:636-694). These must be fixed before item 1 is added, or the new switch would act on wrong verdicts. (Rows 25, 28, 36.)
7. **The boiler status text differs in many small ways.** It is evaluated every 30 s instead of on every change. It never shows `OFF` or "heating hot water", and its thresholds differ (SATcontrol.ino:516-647, device/status.py:34-116). Neither code uses this text to control the boiler (sensor.py:470-487, SATcontrol.ino:2758-2759). (Rows 9 to 17.)

### Not found in the code / unclear

1. **Thermo-nova starts-per-hour limit: intended or not?** The ON-phase count is compared with `self._config.duty_cycle_seconds` (pwm.py:156), a duration in seconds (entry_data.py:220, default 780 per const.py:167), not with `cycles_per_hour` (entry_data.py:219). The code does not show whether this comparison is intended. If it was meant to be `cycles_per_hour`, the thermo-nova limit would be 4 per hour by default (const.py:119). Settled for the firmware by Sergeant D entry 59 (2026-10-25): the firmware counts PWM ON phases only against cycles per hour (TASK-1401); the thermo-nova intent itself stays unknown.
2. **Firmware `loop()` rate.** The 3-sample minimum and the hot water fraction count `loop()` passes (SATcycles.ino:917-920). The loop rate is not set in the code read.
3. **PWM evaluation cadence vs. ignition delay.** Row 44 depends on how soon after an ON-phase start the firmware PWM state machine runs again (SATcontrol.ino:808-832), compared with a real boiler's ignition delay. Neither was measured.
4. **Tail sort defect.** Row 25 was derived from reading the code (SATcycles.ino:525-533). It was not compiled, unit-tested or run on a device, so the actual stack damage is unknown.
5. **OTDirect boards.** OTDirect formats each frame and hands it to the same parser, `processOT()` (OTDirect.ino:721-724). The full path from there to `print_status` for MsgID 0 (OTGW-Core.ino:4548) was not traced line by line.
6. **Thermo-nova central-heating input differs per backend.** OTGW MQTT carries the slave CH bit (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:23, OTGW-Core.ino:2440); the serial backend uses `DATA_MASTER_CH_ENABLED` (coordinator/serial/__init__.py:53-59). So thermo-nova's own `OFF` status and cycle kind depend on the backend.
7. **Intended `POST_CYCLE_SETTLING` behaviour.** Thermo-nova codes it but cannot reach it (row 14, derived from the order of the code); the firmware reaches it (SATcontrol.ino:559-563). The code does not show which was intended.
8. **Deliberate deviations.** The 2.0 °C margin and the 28.2 / 21 °C cold setpoints carry comments that suggest deliberate choices (SATtypes.h:454, SATcontrol.ino:262-263). No ADR recording these deviations from thermo-nova was looked up for this comparison.

---

## Areas, presets, window detection and thermostat sync

Comparison of thermo-nova (ground truth: Alexwijn/SAT `releases/thermo-nova` at 7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb, paths relative to `custom_components/sat`) with the OTGW firmware (`src/OTGW-firmware`, `dev` at 51df6b486). Inputs: `a6-areas-presets-thermonova.md` and `a6-areas-presets-firmware.md`. Every citation in the table was opened again for this comparison. Worked examples marked "computed" follow only from the cited formulas; they were not run.

**Adversarial verification pass.** Every row was checked again by opening both cited locations, and an attempt was made to refute each verdict (other firmware call sites, dead or conditional thermo-nova paths, wrong citations). Firmware lines were read in the working tree at 2c194a329; `git diff 51df6b486 2c194a329` changes no line counts in any cited firmware file (only banner lines; `OTGW-firmware.h` shifted but is not cited). A verdict marked "(verified)" held up. No verdict had to be changed or removed. Rows 3, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10, 11, 23, 26, 27, 31, 35, 38, 39, 52 and 56 had their description, impact or citations corrected or completed.

**Read this first: the word "area" means different things.** In thermo-nova an "area" is a room with its own target, its own heating curve and its own PID; the room results are combined into one flow setpoint (area.py:37-50, climate.py:382-389). In the firmware that role is played by **zones** (SATcontrol.ino:1806-1867, SATcontrol.ino:4846-4896). The firmware's own "multi-area" feature is something else: a weighted average of up to 4 room temperatures that feeds the single main PID (SATcontrol.ino:1217-1222, SATcontrol.ino:1696-1719). The table compares thermo-nova areas with firmware zones, and lists firmware multi-area as firmware-only.

| Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Room model | Each room is a Home Assistant climate entity with its own heating curve and own PID (area.py:37-50, area.py:218-220). | Rooms with own PID are "zones": up to 4 pushed (room temperature, setpoint) pairs (SATcontrol.ino:1726-1729, SATcontrol.ino:1753-1784). | differs (verified) | Same idea, different input path and naming. Easy to confuse with firmware "multi-area". | Rename or document: firmware zones = thermo-nova areas. |
| 2. Weighted room average ("multi-area") | No such feature. The nearest thing, `Area.weight` (0 to 2), is defined (area.py:148-163) but a grep for `.weight` found no caller. | Weighted average of up to 4 area temperatures replaces the main room temperature, ahead of external and OT `Tr`, after BLE (SATcontrol.ino:1206-1222, SATcontrol.ino:1696-1719). Weight default 1.0, range 0 to 10 (SATtypes.h:509, settingStuff.ino:1094-1097). | firmware-only (verified) | Changes which room temperature the main PID regulates on. | Ask Sergeant D whether to keep it as a firmware extension; document it as non-SAT. |
| 3. When rooms take part | Whenever rooms are configured and the heating mode is COMFORT (the default); in ECO the rooms are skipped (climate.py:377-389, area.py:218-220, const.py:183). | Only when `iZoneCount > 1`; default 1, so zones are off by default (SATcontrol.ino:4846, SATtypes.h:531). | differs (verified) | With default settings the firmware ignores pushed zone values. | Document; consider enabling the zone path as soon as a zone sends data. |
| 4. Room inputs | Current temperature from the climate attribute or an override sensor; target from the climate `temperature` attribute; optional valve position (area.py:59-108, area.py:131-146). | Pushed over MQTT only, `sat/zone/<n>/room_temp` and `sat/zone/<n>/setpoint` (MQTTstuff.ino:895-907, SATcontrol.ino:1753-1784); no REST caller of these handlers was found by grep. In simulation mode the firmware synthesizes missing zone values itself (SATcontrol.ino:3940-3960). No valve position. | differs (verified) | Firmware cannot use valve positions (see row 12). | Document the MQTT contract; no change unless valve support is wanted. |
| 5. Room staleness | Source older than `sensor_max_value_age` (default 06:00:00, age measured from the state's `last_updated`) gives no temperature, so the room's PID update is skipped (area.py:76-84, area.py:202-206, const.py:170, helpers.py:59-69). The room is not dropped: `pid.available` depends only on the last error and the curve (pid.py:56-59), so its frozen PID output keeps counting in the aggregate (area.py:278-289). Only the overshoot cap skips it (area.py:312-313). | Zone older than `iZoneTimeoutS` (default 300 s) is excluded and its integral cleared; one shared timestamp for both values (SATcontrol.ino:1764, SATcontrol.ino:1781, SATcontrol.ino:1814-1818, SATcontrol.ino:1786-1796, SATtypes.h:532). | differs (verified) | Firmware drops a quiet zone after 5 min and loses its integral. thermo-nova updates the room normally for up to 6 h; after that the room's last PID output keeps counting with no time limit (its curve can still move with the outside temperature). | Decide with Sergeant D which timeout is wanted; separate timestamps per value. |
| 6. Room PID update rate | Own 60 s timer per room (area.py:188-190, pid.py:20). | Every control tick (default 30 s), but the integral step assumes 60 s (SATcontrol.ino:4853-4854, SATcontrol.ino:1846, SATtypes.h:444). | differs (verified) | With a 30 s tick the zone integral grows twice as fast in real time (computed). | Step the zone PID on a 60 s timer, or scale the step to the real interval. |
| 7. Room PID terms | Same PID class as the main thermostat: output = curve + P + I + D, rounded to 0.1 (pid.py:127-132, area.py:50). Automatic Kd = 0.07 * 8400 * Kp (pid.py:85-93). | Output = curve + P + I, no D term (SATcontrol.ino:1855-1858). | differs (verified) | Firmware zones have no damping on a fast temperature change. | Use the main PID implementation for zones. |
| 8. Room PID gains | Automatic (Kp = coefficient * curve / 4 or 3, Ki = Kp / 8400) or manual from config (pid.py:62-82). | Always the automatic formula (SATcontrol.ino:1834-1837), also when the main PID uses manual gains (`bAutoGains` false, SATtypes.h:518-521). | differs (verified) | Only matters with manual gains. | Follow the main PID gain mode. |
| 9. Room integral | Outside `DEADBAND` (0.1) reset to 0; inside add `ki * error * 60`, clamp to plus or minus the curve value (pid.py:185-192, helpers.py:148-150, const.py:16). | Same, plus an extra clamp to plus or minus 20; deadband is the setting `fDeadband`, default 0.1 (SATcontrol.ino:1840-1853, SATtypes.h:443). | differs (verified) | The 20 °C clamp is the tighter one whenever the curve value is above 20 °C, which is usual for radiators (base offset 27.2, SATcontrol.ino:50). Reaching it takes long: with coefficient 1.5 and curve 40 °C the step is at most about 0.014 °C per tick, so roughly 1400 ticks (about 12 h at 30 s) inside the deadband (computed). Small in practice. | Remove the extra clamp or document it. |
| 10. Room PID memory | Loaded from storage at start and saved after every update (pid.py:141-157, pid.py:179-181, area.py:177-181). | RAM only (SATcontrol.ino:1726). The SAT PID file stores only the main PID values (SATcontrol.ino:1995-2005), and a grep for `satZones[` found no save or load (its only other users are the simulation and the overshoot input, SATcontrol.ino:3944, SATcontrol.ino:4858). | differs (verified) | After a reboot every zone integral starts at 0. | Persist zone PID state, or accept and document. |
| 11. Room PID reset on a room target change | On a state event of a room that is in `_rooms`, when its target differs from the stored value, that room's PID (integral and derivative) is reset and the curves are recalculated (climate.py:775-798, area.py:335-338, pid.py:134-137). The stored value is only updated when the room is new or the preset is `home` (climate.py:791-792). Outside `home`, once a room's target differs from the stored value, every later state event of that room resets its PID again, also an event that only changes its current temperature (computed from the code, not run). | A zone setpoint change does not reset the zone PID (SATcontrol.ino:1770-1784). | differs (verified) | Firmware can carry an old integral into a new zone target, but the integral is cleared anyway once the error leaves the deadband (SATcontrol.ino:1840-1853). thermo-nova, outside `home`, can keep a changed room's integral and derivative near zero by resetting on every event. | Reset the zone PID on a setpoint change; ask Sergeant D whether the repeated reset in thermo-nova is intended. |

Which rooms count ("requires heat") | PID available, and valve position >= 10.0, or without valve PID output > 22 °C (`COLD_SETPOINT`) (area.py:165-175, area.py:278-280, pid.py:56-59, const.py:34). | Zone output > 10 °C (SATcontrol.ino:4855). | differs (verified) | Firmware counts rooms with a low demand (output 10 to 22 °C) that thermo-nova ignores; this changes the percentile pick and the overshoot input. | Use the thermo-nova test (22 °C threshold, valve when known). |

Aggregation: headroom | Headroom is a limit: `chosen = min(highest, baseline + 5.0)`; never above the hottest room (area.py:267, area.py:296-299). | Headroom is added: `aggregate = picked + headroom` (default 5.0) (SATcontrol.ino:4882, SATtypes.h:533). | differs (verified) | Large. Firmware asks 5 °C too much with 2 or 3 rooms; with 4 rooms it can ask more or less than thermo-nova, depending on the spread, and can under-serve the room with the highest demand. | Implement `min(highest, baseline + headroom)`. |

### Most important differences, in plain words

1. **Rooms are combined differently.** thermo-nova takes the room demand at the 75 % point, may add up to 5 °C, but never goes above the highest room demand. The firmware always adds the 5 °C on top. With two or three rooms the firmware asks the boiler for 5 °C more than SAT would. With four rooms it can be more or less (rows 13, 14).
2. **The main thermostat can be ignored.** thermo-nova uses whichever is higher, main room or rooms. Once zones are active, the firmware throws away the main room's demand (row 17).
3. **Too-warm rooms are handled differently.** thermo-nova puts a hard ceiling on the flow temperature (for example 28 °C when a room is 1 °C too warm), as long as that room still counts as calling for heat. The firmware only lowers the flow temperature by the same number of degrees as the excess, so by 1 °C, and only when 2 or more zones are active (row 18).
4. **Low-demand rooms count in the firmware.** In thermo-nova, a room that reports a valve position counts only when the valve is at 10 or more. A room without a valve position counts only when its PID asks more than 22 °C. The firmware counts any zone whose PID asks more than 10 °C (row 12).
5. **ECO mode does nothing in the firmware** (row 19).
6. **Window detection is slower and off by default.** Default 60 s instead of 15 s, checked only every control tick (30 s), and paused during hot water. On close, the firmware always restores, also over a change made while the window was open (rows 45, 46, 51).
7. **Preset defaults differ** (away 15 instead of 10, sleep 16 instead of 15, comfort 21 instead of 20; extra `eco`). A manual temperature leaves the old preset name visible (rows 28, 32).
8. **Less is reset and less is saved.** The firmware does not reset the room PIDs on a target change, keeps zone memory in RAM only, and after a reboot forgets the preset. A preset or window target reaches flash only with the next unrelated settings save (rows 10, 36, 37, 38).
9. **Thermostat following has its own design.** It reads the wall thermostat from the OpenTherm bus, filters noise and SAT's own echo, and clears the preset. thermo-nova follows a Home Assistant thermostat entity and keeps the preset (row 41).
10. **Quiet rooms behave differently.** The firmware drops a zone after 5 minutes without data and clears its integral. thermo-nova never drops a room: after 6 hours without data it stops updating the room's PID but keeps counting its last output (row 5).

### Not found in the code / unclear

1. Firmware zone OFF flag `bOff`: read at SATcontrol.ino:1814, SATcontrol.ino:1885 and SATcontrol.ino:3945, but a grep of `src/OTGW-firmware` found no line that sets it to true (SATtypes.h:139). Not reachable from outside.
2. thermo-nova `push_setpoint_to_thermostat`: read from `data` (entry_data.py:154-155), while the options form writes the key to the options (config_flow.py:670, config_flow.py:804-806). A grep found no other writer apart from a migration (__init__.py:187). From the code read, the options toggle has no effect; not tested.
3. What heating control does with thermo-nova's `valves_open` in `HeatingDemand` is outside this section (climate.py:547-555). Row 24 therefore compares only the input and the firmware's direct action.
4. thermo-nova preset `none` with no saved value sets the target to none (climate.py:613-616, climate.py:643-647). Whether this happens in practice depends on Home Assistant restore behaviour, not shown here.
5. What the PIC or OT-Direct does with `TC=` and `TT=` is not in the code read. Whether a `TT=` from the firmware's Home Assistant entity ever reaches SAT's target is not shown; the follow path reads only thermostat T frames (OTGW-Core.ino:5214-5217).
6. thermo-nova start-up gap on an integration reload (rooms count for nothing until the first heating curve update) follows from reading climate.py:133-148 and area.py:179; it was not run.
7. thermo-nova `Area.async_outside_entity_changed` (area.py:198-200) updates a room's curve with the room's own current temperature in the outside-temperature argument. A grep found no code that registers or calls it, so it looks like dead code; not run.
8. The unit of thermo-nova `current_valve_position` is not stated; the code only compares it with 10.0 (area.py:168-170).
9. Row 11: whether a Home Assistant state event also fires when only the room's current temperature changes is Home Assistant behaviour, not code in this repository. The repeated-reset reading assumes it does.
10. The maximum setpoint, PID internals of the main loop, PWM, solar gain, comfort offset and PV boost belong to other sections and are not compared here.

---

## Solar gain, summer simmer, temperature history and health sensors

Comparison of thermo-nova (ground truth: Alexwijn/SAT `releases/thermo-nova`, commit `7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb`, paths relative to `custom_components/sat`) with the OTGW firmware (branch `dev`, commit `51df6b486`, paths relative to `src/OTGW-firmware`). Built from `a7-features-thermonova.md` and `a7-features-firmware.md`. Every row was checked against the code itself, not only against those two documents. A citation marked "(search)" means a text search over the source tree, not a single line.

Adversarial verification pass (2026-10-22): every row below was reopened on both sides. " (verified)" after a verdict means both cited locations were read in that pass and the verdict held. Two verdicts changed (T4, T9: match to differs). Several cells were corrected with the verdict kept (W3, K1, H3, H6, P4, P5, P7, Y4, Y5, E1, T2, T8, S7, plus citation fixes).

How to read the verdict column:

- **match**: same behaviour.
- **differs**: both have the feature, but it behaves differently.
- **missing in firmware**: thermo-nova has it, the firmware does not.
- **firmware-only**: the firmware has it, thermo-nova does not.

Timing note for the whole table. Thermo-nova runs solar gain and history once per PID tick of 60 s (climate.py:712-716, pid.py:20). The firmware runs everything once per control tick, default 30 s (SATtypes.h:444, SATcontrol.ino:4614).

### Comparison table

#### Solar gain compensation

| # | Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | Defaults | Off by default. Minimum sun elevation 12°, minimum rise 0.6 °C/h, setpoint offset 2.0 °C, freeze integral on (const.py:132-136). | Same values: off, 0.6 °C/h, 2.0 °C, 12°, freeze on (SATtypes.h:516-519, SATtypes.h:549 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]). | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| S2 | Setting ranges | Elevation -10 to 90°, rise 0.0 to 4.0 °C/h, offset 0.0 to 10.0 °C (config_flow.py:738-749). | Rise 0.1 to 5.0, offset 0.5 to 10.0, elevation -10 to 45, values outside are clamped (settingStuff.ino:1083-1085, settingStuff.ino:707-715). | differs (verified) | Small. In the firmware you cannot set an offset of 0, a rise of 0, or an elevation above 45°. | Align the ranges with thermo-nova. |
| S3 | How often a temperature sample is taken | Once per 60 s PID tick (climate.py:484-494, climate.py:712-716). | On a control tick (default 30 s), but only when more than 30 s have passed since the last sample (SATcontrol.ino:4235, SATtypes.h:444). | differs (verified) | The rise rate is measured over a different time step, so the same room behaviour gives different numbers. | Sample every 60 s, as thermo-nova does. |
| S4 | Indoor rise rate | Plain slope between two samples, no smoothing: `(T_new - T_prev) * 3600 / dt` (solar_gain.py:74-78). | Raw slope, then averaged: `ema = 0.3 * raw + 0.7 * ema`, starting from 0 (SATcontrol.ino:4236-4238, SATcontrol.ino:4211). | differs (verified) | The firmware sees a real rise later. It needs several samples before the average passes the threshold. | Use the unsmoothed slope. |
| S5 | Sun elevation: source and missing data | Read from the Home Assistant `sun.sun` entity (climate.py:504-513). Without an elevation, solar gain is off and any hold is cleared (solar_gain.py:56-58). | Only from an MQTT push, accepted -90 to 90° (MQTTstuff.ino:618, SATcontrol.ino:1650-1662). Without data, or with data older than 1 h, detection runs on rise rate alone: "rely on rise rate alone" (SATcontrol.ino:4468-4482 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]). | differs (verified) | Large. Without sun data the firmware can lower the setpoint because of any indoor warming (people, cooking, a stove), also at night. Thermo-nova never does that. | Require a valid sun elevation, as thermo-nova does. If no sun source is wanted, Sergeant D decides; then document the deviation. |
| S6 | Sun below the minimum elevation | No new event, but a running hold expires on its own, up to 600 s later (solar_gain.py:63, solar_gain.py:84-85). | Active flag cleared at once (SATcontrol.ino:4254-4259). | differs (verified) | Small. At sunset the firmware stops compensating up to 10 min earlier. | Follow the event and hold logic (see S10, S11). |
| S7 | Heating mode gate | Off and hold cleared when the HVAC mode is not HEAT (solar_gain.py:52-54, climate.py:491). | No mode check inside the solar code (SATcontrol.ino:4215-4299). It only runs on control ticks that reach it (SATcontrol.ino:4762). When SAT is switched off the tick returns before the solar code (SATcontrol.ino:4568-4572) and `satDisable()` does not touch the solar state (SATcontrol.ino:2160-2187, search), so the last solar state is kept. | differs (verified) | Low. The firmware has no separate HVAC mode in this path. | Note only. |
| S8 | "Boiler output is low" | Flame off counts as low. Flame on counts as low only with a known modulation of 20 % or less (solar_gain.py:95-103, const.py:31). | Reported relative modulation below 20 %, flame not checked (SATcontrol.ino:4267-4269). The value is 0.0 before the boiler reports one (OTGW-Core.h:72). | differs (verified) | Exactly 20 % counts as low in thermo-nova, not in the firmware. Flame on with unknown modulation counts as low in the firmware (0.0), not in thermo-nova. | Add the flame check and use "20 % or less". |
| S9 | Valves open | Required (solar_gain.py:90-91). True when no radiators are configured (climate.py:420-422). | Required (SATcontrol.ino:4270). Default true when no valve data arrives (SATtypes.h:243). | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| S10 | When compensation starts | On the first tick that meets all conditions; a hold of 600 s starts at once (solar_gain.py:60-63, const.py:24). | All conditions must hold for more than 600 s in a row before it starts (SATcontrol.ino:4489-4501 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]). | differs (verified) | Large. The firmware starts at least 10 min later, on top of the averaging delay of S4. The room overshoots more before the setpoint drops. | Use the thermo-nova event plus hold. |
| S11 | When compensation ends | 600 s after the last event. Each event needs all conditions again (sun, rise, valves, low output) to extend the hold (solar_gain.py:60-63, solar_gain.py:80-93). | Stays on while the averaged rise rate is above the threshold. Ends after more than 600 s without that. Modulation and valves are not checked while active (SATcontrol.ino:4285-4298). | differs (verified) | The firmware keeps lowering the setpoint even when the boiler runs hard or valves close, as long as the room is still rising. | Use the thermo-nova hold rule. |
| S12 | Where the offset is applied | Subtracted from the requested setpoint, before heating control decides PWM or continuous output (climate.py:391-394, climate.py:547-555). | Subtracted from the requested setpoint before the clamp and before PWM or continuous control (SATcontrol.ino:5137-5152); the 3 s PWM refresh has no solar term (`satPwmSuppressionRefresh`, SATcontrol.ino:1045-1065); the auto-switch sees the value after the offset (SATcontrol.ino:5542-5546) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351); TASK-1323. | match (corrected) | None: in both the lower request flows into the PWM and continuous decisions. | Done (TASK-1323, entry 11). |
| S13 | Can the offset switch the boiler off? | Yes. If the lowered request is 22 °C or less, heater demand is OFF: control setpoint 10 °C, configured maximum modulation, heater OFF (heating_control.py:64-72, heating_control.py:202, heating_control.py:229-238, const.py:34-35). | Yes. The offset lowers the requested setpoint before the cold cutoff, so a lowered request at or below 28.2 °C (radiators) or 21.0 °C (underfloor) gives CS 10 °C, CH 0, the configured maximum MM and PWM off (SATcontrol.ino:5137-5152, SATcontrol.ino:5206-5214, SATchEnable.h:26-52) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351); TASK-1323. | match (corrected) | Both can stop the boiler on strong sun; only the threshold differs. The cutoff value itself (22 vs 28.2/21) belongs to the heating control section. | Done (TASK-1323, entry 11); the threshold 28.2 / 21 is Sergeant D's choice (entries 7 and 11). |
| S14 | ECO heating mode | ECO returns before the offset line, so no solar offset in ECO (climate.py:377-379). | No ECO exception (SATcontrol.ino:5141-5142 [lines refreshed 2026-10-24, TASK-1351]). The ECO setting is stored (SATtypes.h:524) but only read for settings, REST and MQTT output, not by control code (search: SATcontrol.ino:2533, SATcontrol.ino:2978, restAPI.ino:2315, restAPI.ino:4021, settingStuff.ino:387, settingStuff.ino:1104, handleDebug.ino:119). | differs (verified) | Only relevant once the firmware implements ECO; see the heating mode section. | Skip the offset in ECO when ECO is implemented. |
| S15 | How the integral is frozen | The whole integral update is skipped (pid.py:165-171). So the reset to 0 for an error beyond 0.1 °C is skipped too (pid.py:185-189, const.py:16). The integral survives a sun-driven overshoot. | The whole integral update is skipped, the reset outside the deadband included, when the freeze setting is on (SATpid.ino:157) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351); TASK-1323, entry 11 answer 3. | match (corrected) | None: in both the stored integral is still there when the sun goes. | Done (TASK-1323, entry 11 answer 3). |
| S16 | Freeze-integral setting | Freeze only when the setting is on (climate.py:499-502). | Freeze only while solar gain is active and `bSolarFreezeIntegral` is on (SATpid.ino:157, SATtypes.h:549) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351); TASK-1323. | match (corrected) | None. | Done (TASK-1323). |
| S17 | Memory across a restart | None. Controller starts empty (solar_gain.py:43-46). | None. Static RAM values start at 0 (SATcontrol.ino:4209-4213). | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| S18 | Values shown to the user | `solar_gain_active`, `solar_gain_sun_elevation`, `solar_gain_rise_per_hour` (climate.py:253-255). | `sat/solar_gain`, `sat/indoor_rise_rate`, `sat/solar_gain_sun_elevation`; the elevation only while it is valid (SATcontrol.ino:2943-2947). | match (verified) | Display only. The rise rate value differs because of S4. | None beyond S4. |

#### Summer simmer index, thermal comfort and firmware-only humidity features

| # | Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | Index formula | `1.98 * (F - (0.55 - 0.0055 * H) * (F - 58)) - 56.83`; below 58 °F the index is the temperature (summer_simmer.py:26-35). | Same formula and pivot (SATcontrol.ino:2579-2582). | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| T2 | Rounding of the index | Rounded to 0.1 °C (summer_simmer.py:38). The PID input can differ by up to 0.05 °C, which is half the 0.1 °C deadband. | Round to 0.1 °C. |
| T3 | Out-of-range humidity | No range check; only `None` gives `None` (summer_simmer.py:22-23). | Humidity below 0 or above 100 returns the plain temperature (SATcontrol.ino:2578); input is also rejected outside 0 to 100 (SATcontrol.ino:1641). | firmware-only (verified) | Defensive guard; no effect with valid input. | Keep. |
| T4 | Perception labels | Nine bands from "Cool" to "Circulatory Collapse Imminent", plus "Unknown" without data (summer_simmer.py:44-63). The band is chosen from the index rounded to 0.1 °C (summer_simmer.py:38, summer_simmer.py:42). | Same nine bands and thresholds (SATcontrol.ino:2585-2595), but chosen from the unrounded index (SATcontrol.ino:2582, SATcontrol.ino:3015-3018). Without valid humidity (or with humidity 0) the topics are not published instead of "Unknown" (SATcontrol.ino:3014). | differs (verified) | Display only. Near a band edge the label can differ: 21.06 °C is "Cool" in the firmware and "Slightly Cool" in thermo-nova (rounded to 21.1). | Classify the rounded index (fixed together with T2); publish "Unknown" without data. |
| T5 | Thermal comfort default | Off (const.py:131). | Off (SATtypes.h:522). | match (verified) | None. | None. |
| T6 | Which room temperature is converted | The configured inside sensor, whatever it is (climate.py:271-279). | Only the OpenTherm room temperature (MsgID 24). BLE, multi-area and MQTT external temperatures return earlier and are never converted (SATcontrol.ino:1208-1231, SATcontrol.ino:1264-1270). | differs (verified) | With a BLE or external sensor, switching thermal comfort on does nothing in the firmware. | Apply the index to the selected room source. |
| T7 | Humidity missing or stale | Index is `None`, so the room temperature is `None`, so the PID tick is skipped (climate.py:276-277, climate.py:305-306, climate.py:480-482). A sensor older than `sensor_max_value_age` (default 6 h) counts as missing (climate.py:669-686, const.py:170). | Falls back to the raw room temperature and keeps controlling (SATcontrol.ino:1264-1272). Stale after `iHumidityTimeoutS`, default 1800 s (SATtypes.h:523). | differs (verified) | Thermo-nova stops updating the PID; the firmware keeps heating on the raw temperature. | Sergeant D decides which is intended. Then align or document. |
| T8 | Humidity source | A Home Assistant humidity sensor (device class HUMIDITY) (climate.py:281-287, config_flow.py:392-397). | MQTT or REST push (SATcontrol.ino:1635-1647, MQTTstuff.ino:617, restAPI.ino:1556). If none is valid, the weather service humidity is copied in (SATcontrol.ino:4816-4822). That is outdoor humidity: Open-Meteo `relative_humidity_2m` (SATweather.ino:63, SATweather.ino:70, SATweather.ino:261). BLE humidity is kept per BLE slot (SATble.ino:597) and never reaches `state.sat.fHumidity`, whose only writers are SATcontrol.ino:1642 and SATcontrol.ino:4819 (search). | differs (verified) | With only weather data, the firmware corrects the indoor temperature with outdoor humidity. | Do not use outdoor humidity for thermal comfort, or let Sergeant D approve it. |
| T9 | Published index with thermal comfort on | Computed from `current_temperature`, which is already the index, so it is the index of the index (climate.py:245, climate.py:276-277). | Same quirk only when the room temperature comes from MsgID 24: `satGetRoomTemp()` then already returns the index (SATcontrol.ino:3015, SATcontrol.ino:1264-1270). With BLE, multi-area or external temperature it returns the raw value (SATcontrol.ino:1208-1231), so the firmware publishes a single index there. | differs (verified) | Display only. Thermo-nova carries the quirk for every source; the firmware only for MsgID 24. | None beyond T6; if T6 is aligned, the quirk becomes the same. |
| T10 | Summer mode (`bSummerSimmer`) | No such feature. In thermo-nova "summer simmer" is only the comfort index (search for "summer" outside `summer_simmer`/`SummerSimmer`: no hits). | When the outside temperature stays at or above `fSummerThreshold` (18 °C) for `iSummerMinHours` (6 h), the tick sends CS=10, CH=0 and skips the rest, including the PID (SATcontrol.ino:4307-4345, SATcontrol.ino:4764-4776, SATtypes.h:498-501). | firmware-only (verified) | Large when enabled: heating stops. Off by default (SATtypes.h:499). The shared name invites confusion with the index. | Sergeant D decides whether it stays. If it stays, rename it so it is not confused with the comfort index. |
| T11 | Humidity comfort offset (`bComfortAdjust`) | No such feature (search for "comfort" finds only the presets, `thermal_comfort` and an area band: area.py:31, climate.py:276, const.py:62). | Shifts the room target by up to ±`fComfortMaxOffset` (1.0 °C) based on humidity vs 50 %. It only works when the summer mode setting is also on (SATcontrol.ino:3222-3246, SATcontrol.ino:4831-4839). | firmware-only (verified) | Changes the room target used by the curve and the PID. Off by default (SATtypes.h:503). | Sergeant D decides whether it stays. |

#### Temperature history and heating curve recommendation

| # | Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | Room error and "Error Value" | `error = round(setpoint - current, 3)`, positive = room too cold (temperature/state.py:15-18); shown as sensor "Error Value" (sensor.py:384-389). | `round((target - room) * 1000) / 1000` (SATpid.ino:63-66); stored in `fError` (SATpid.ino:305) and published as `sat/error` (SATcontrol.ino:2737). | match (verified) | Display only. | None. |
| H2 | Value stored in the history | Median of the primary room error and every area error (climate.py:396-412). | The single PID error `fError` (SATcontrol.ino:3507). No median over areas. | differs (verified) | Advice only. Differs only with several areas. | Use the median over rooms. |
| H3 | When a sample is taken | Every 60 s tick, only in HEAT with open valves (climate.py:496-497). | Once per hour while SAT is active (SATcontrol.ino:3497-3504). The call is only reached when the summer-mode and valves-closed returns did not fire (SATcontrol.ino:4766-4790), so also only with valves open. The sample is taken before this tick's PID update, so it is one tick old (SATcontrol.ino:4796, SATcontrol.ino:4839). | differs (verified) | Advice only. About 60 times fewer samples. | Sample every 60 s. |
| H4 | Storage window | Rolling 24 h of samples, with a 4 h and a 24 h window; window end is the newest sample (temperature/history.py:10-11, temperature/history.py:59-62, temperature/history.py:85-100). | Ring of 24 hourly samples (SATcontrol.ino:3488-3493). The firmware's other 4 h and 24 h windows hold boiler cycle statistics, not room errors (SATcycles.ino:303-397, SATcycles.ino:436-475). | differs (verified) | Advice only. | Store 24 h of 60 s samples. |
| H5 | Recommendation rule | Needs 6 samples in 24 h. Uses the **median** of the 24 h window: above +0.2 °C INCREASE, below -0.2 °C DECREASE, else HOLD (sensor.py:300-333). | Needs 6 hourly samples. Uses the **mean**: same ±0.2 °C threshold (SATcontrol.ino:3513-3544). | differs (verified) | Advice only. A mean reacts to single outliers; a median does not. Thermo-nova needs 6 minutes of data, the firmware 6 hours. | Use the median of the 24 h window. |
| H6 | Statistics published | Mean, median, mean absolute error and in-band fraction (error within 0.1 °C), for the 4 h and 24 h windows (temperature/history.py:64-108, sensor.py:335-356). | Mean and standard deviation of the hourly ring, as `sat/error_mean` and `sat/error_stddev` (SATcontrol.ino:3520-3534, SATcontrol.ino:2919-2920). An attribute message adds the sample count and a field named `recent_mean_error` that holds the current PID error, not a mean (SATcontrol.ino:2908-2914). | differs (verified) | Display only. The name `recent_mean_error` suggests the thermo-nova 4 h mean but carries a different value. | Publish the thermo-nova set; fix or drop the `recent_mean_error` field. |
| H7 | Daily-median recommendation | No such mechanism; the only recommendation is H5 (sensor.py:299-333). | Second recommendation: one median per UTC day, kept for 30 days and saved to flash. INCREASE or DECREASE when the last 3 daily medians are all beyond ±0.5 °C. Opposite sign convention: positive = room too warm (SATcycles.ino:165-166, SATcycles.ino:1201-1289, ../libraries/Platform/src/boards.h:123-124). | firmware-only (verified) | Advice only. Two recommendations can disagree. | Sergeant D decides whether it stays; if so, give it a clearly different name. |

#### Pressure health

| # | Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | Verdict | Impact on heating behaviour | Proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Core constants | Smoothing factor 0.05, confirmation 120 s, drop-rate pause 600 s, minimum span 300 s and 3 samples (binary_sensor.py:25-28, binary_sensor.py:315-323, binary_sensor.py:368-373). Defaults 0.8 bar, 2.5 bar, 0.3 bar/h (const.py:157-159). | Same values (SATcontrol.ino:3349, SATcontrol.ino:3362-3367, SATcontrol.ino:3405, SATcontrol.ino:3414, SATcontrol.ino:3467; SATtypes.h:469-471). | match (verified) | None (display only). | None. |
| P2 | Setting ranges | Minimum and maximum 0 to 4 bar, drop rate 0 to 3 bar/h (config_flow.py:781-792). | Minimum 0 to 3, maximum 1 to 4, drop rate 0.05 to 2, values outside are clamped (settingStuff.ino:1041-1043). | differs (verified) | Display only. | Align the ranges. |
| P3 | Samples used for the drop rate | Every reading kept for at least 3600 s (or the maximum age if longer) (binary_sensor.py:357-365). | 20-slot ring, one sample per 30 s at most, so roughly the last 10 min (SATcontrol.ino:3326, SATcontrol.ino:3371-3398). | differs (verified) | Display only. The firmware rate is based on a much shorter period, so it is noisier. | Keep about 1 h of samples. |
| P4 | What pauses the drop-rate check | A change of the coordinator `active` flag (binary_sensor.py:335-345). Its meaning depends on the backend: MQTT OpenTherm uses `central_heating == ON` (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:60-61), serial uses "master CH enabled" (coordinator/serial/__init__.py:54-55); the abstract docstring says "actively heating" (coordinator/__init__.py:158-160). | A change of SAT's own active flag (SATcontrol.ino:3343-3355), which is set on every control tick (SATcontrol.ino:4616). | differs (verified) | Display only. In thermo-nova the pause follows the boiler's CH flag; in the firmware it follows only SAT being switched on or off, so pressure swings at CH on/off are not paused and can give false drop alarms (derived). | Pause on CH on/off changes. |
| P5 | Alarm when readings stop | Problem when the last reading is older than the maximum age, default 30 min (binary_sensor.py:264-271, const.py:160). | No such alarm. Readings below 0.01 bar are ignored and the state stays as it was (SATcontrol.ino:3358). `iLastSeenPressureMs` is written (SATcontrol.ino:3360) but never read (search). | missing in firmware (verified) | Display only. A lost pressure signal goes unnoticed. | Add the stale-reading alarm. |
| P6 | Memory across a restart | Restores last pressure, timestamps and smoothed value (binary_sensor.py:215-229). | Nothing restored: the ring and smoothed value live only in RAM (SATcontrol.ino:3326-3336, SATtypes.h:253; search for `fSmoothedPressure` finds no persistence). | missing in firmware (verified) | Display only. | Optional; low priority. |
| P7 | Meaning of "ON" | Binary sensor of class PROBLEM: on = problem (binary_sensor.py:237-239, binary_sensor.py:295). | `sat/pressure_health` "ON" = healthy (SATcontrol.ino:2886, SATcontrol.ino:3468-3479). A second topic `sat/pressure_alarm` has the thermo-nova sense, true = problem (SATcontrol.ino:2884). | differs (verified) | Display only, but `pressure_health` is inverted: a user can read "ON" as a fault. | Use ON = problem for `pressure_health`, as thermo-nova does, or drop it in favour of `pressure_alarm`. |

### Most important differences, in plain words

1. **Solar gain switches on too late and off at the wrong time** (S4, S10, S11). Thermo-nova reacts on the first minute the room warms from the sun and keeps that for 10 minutes after the last sign of sun. The firmware first averages the warming, then waits another 10 minutes before it acts, and then keeps acting while the room still warms, even if the boiler runs hard.
2. **Solar gain works without the sun** (S5). Thermo-nova does nothing without a sun position. The firmware then uses only "the room gets warmer", so cooking, a wood stove or guests can lower the boiler setpoint, also at night.
3. **The setpoint reduction is applied at a different place** (S12, S13). Thermo-nova lowers the heat request itself, so the PWM and on/off decisions follow it and strong sun can stop the boiler. The firmware lowers only the number sent to the boiler at the very end.
4. **The integral is frozen as in thermo-nova** (S15, S16). Since TASK-1323 the firmware skips the whole integral update during sun, the reset included, and honours the on/off setting (SATpid.ino:157) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).
5. **Thermal comfort only works with the thermostat's own temperature** (T6, T7, T8, T9). With a BLE or external sensor the setting does nothing. Without humidity the firmware keeps heating on the raw temperature where thermo-nova stops the PID. The firmware can also use outdoor humidity from the weather service. The published index and its label also differ in detail (T2, T4, T9).
6. **Two firmware-only features change heating** (T10, T11). "Summer simmer" in the firmware is a summer mode that turns heating off after warm hours outside, and there is a humidity offset on the target. Neither exists in thermo-nova. Both are off by default.
7. **The heating curve advice uses different maths** (H2 to H6, H7). Thermo-nova uses the median error of the last 24 hours, sampled every minute. The firmware uses an hourly mean, plus a second, firmware-only, multi-day advice with a 0.5 °C threshold and the opposite sign. One published firmware field, `recent_mean_error`, carries the current error instead of a mean.
8. **Health sensors differ in detail** (P3 to P7, Y2, Y4, Y5, E1). "Pressure health ON" means healthy in the firmware but a problem in thermo-nova; the firmware's separate `pressure_alarm` topic has the thermo-nova sense. Lost pressure readings raise no alarm. Several checks use other inputs or tolerances. Most of these sensors do not appear in Home Assistant automatically. None of them changes the heating.

### Verdict counts

| Verdict | Rows |
|---|---|
| match | 11 (S1, S9, S17, S18, T1, T5, H1, P1, Y1, D1, C1) |
| differs | 37 (S2 to S8, S10 to S16, T2, T4, T6, T7, T8, T9, H2 to H6, P2, P3, P4, P7, G1, Y2, Y4, Y5, W1, W2, W3, K1) |
| missing in firmware | 3 (P5, P6, E1) |
| firmware-only | 6 (T3, T10, T11, H7, Y3, F1) |
| **Total** | **57** |

### Not found in the code / unclear

1. **Evaluation rate of the thermo-nova binary sensors.** Their 60 s and 120 s timers only advance when Home Assistant reads the property (binary_sensor.py:76-88, binary_sensor.py:253-295). How often that happens is framework behaviour, not SAT code. So the exact timing comparison in G1 cannot be completed from the code.
2. **Device "active" in thermo-nova for other backends.** For MQTT OpenTherm `active` is `central_heating == ON` (coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py:60-61) and for serial it is "master CH enabled" (coordinator/serial/__init__.py:54-55). Whether `central_heating` in the MQTT OpenTherm backend is the master CH-enable bit or the slave CH-mode bit was not traced. The other backends (esphome, ems, otthing, simulator, switch, fake) were not read for this section.
3. **Intent of the deviations.** No firmware comment explains why sun data is optional (SATcontrol.ino:4262), why activation needs 10 minutes (SATcontrol.ino:4273), or why the summer mode and comfort offset were added (SATtypes.h:498-505). Only the task numbers are given.
