## Boiler I/O, DHW, limits and safety, configuration defaults

### How to read this comparison

- **Ground truth:** thermo-nova, Alexwijn/SAT branch `releases/thermo-nova`, commit 7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb. Thermo-nova citations are relative to `custom_components/sat/`, for example `heating_control.py:236-238`.
- **Firmware:** OTGW-firmware branch `dev`. Firmware citations are relative to `src/OTGW-firmware/`, for example `SATcontrol.ino:4978-4994`.
  - The firmware companion section was written against 51df6b486. The checked-out HEAD is now 2c194a329.
  - Between the two commits, the SAT files cited here changed only in their version banners, one line for one line. The only line shift is in `OTGW-firmware.h` after line 125 (checked with `git diff --numstat` and `git diff -U0`).
  - All firmware citations below are valid at 2c194a329. `OTGW-firmware.h:559-561` is given at 2c194a329.
- **Inputs:** the two companion sections `a8-io-dhw-safety-thermonova.md` and `a8-io-dhw-safety-firmware.md`. The key claims were re-read in the code for this comparison: the boot sequence, the write order, CH and MM rules, the DHW branch, the clamps, the continuous branch, Immergas, the defaults and the safety paths.
- **Which thermo-nova backend is compared.** The firmware is itself an OpenTherm Gateway. So the firmware is compared with thermo-nova's **OTGW via MQTT** coordinator (`coordinator/mqtt/opentherm.py`). The serial (pyotgw) coordinator is named only where it behaves differently, for example in the unload release.
- **Verdicts:**
  - *match*: same behaviour for the boiler.
  - *differs*: both have the behaviour, but it is not the same.
  - *missing in firmware*: thermo-nova has it, the firmware does not.
  - *firmware-only*: the firmware has it, thermo-nova does not.
- **Adversarial check.** Every row whose verdict is not *match*, plus rows 14, 16, 30 and 57, was re-opened at both cited locations and tested for a refutation (firmware doing it elsewhere, a dead or conditional thermo-nova path, a wrong citation). A verdict that held is marked "(verified)". Row 25 changed from *missing in firmware* to *differs*: the gateway's MQTT command `maxchsetpt` sends `SH=`. The firmware files cited here are line-identical between 51df6b486 and 2c194a329 (`git diff -U0 51df6b486 HEAD` shows only one-line-for-one-line changes, none in `SATcontrol.ino` or `SATcycles.ino`).
- **Derived from code, not tested on hardware.** Two firmware findings come from reading the code only: the boot `PM=` collapse on a PIC (row 3) and the skipped first `MM=0`/`CH=0` (row 10). Neither was reproduced on a bench.

### Comparison table

| # | Topic | thermo-nova (citation) | firmware (citation) | verdict | impact on heating behaviour | proposed action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Start-up: backend choice | Eight coordinators, chosen by `mode`: fake, simulator, switch, esphome, mqtt_ems, mqtt_opentherm, mqtt_otthing, serial (`entry_data.py:22-30`, `coordinator/__init__.py:52-87`). | One path. `addCommandToQueue()` sends to the PIC queue, or straight to the OT-Direct handler when there is no PIC (`OTGW-Core.ino:3617-3628`). | missing in firmware (verified) | None. The firmware is the gateway, so only the OTGW backend applies. | None. |
| 2 | Start-up: release of a stale control setpoint | No setpoint is written before the first control tick. All CS, MM and CH writes come from `update()` (`heating_control.py:236-238`), which runs only from the tick (`climate.py:540-555`). | `initSAT()` sends `CS=0` at once when a PIC or OT-Direct exists (`SATcontrol.ino:3702-3710`). This happens before the enabled check, so also with SAT disabled (`SATcontrol.ino:3721`). | firmware-only (verified) | After a reboot the thermostat drives the boiler until the first SAT tick, one full interval later (30 s by default, `SATtypes.h:444`): `initSAT()` sets the tick timer to "now + interval" (`SATcontrol.ino:3719`, `safeTimers.h:131-135`). With SAT disabled, every boot also clears a CS that another controller had set. | Keep as a port-specific safety step. Ask Sergeant D whether sending it with SAT disabled is wanted. |

They are sent twice per start, because `async_added_to_hass` runs from `__init__.py:51` and from `climate.py:147` (`coordinator/mqtt/__init__.py:52-64`). | Sent only when SAT is enabled at boot and a PIC or OT-Direct is already available inside `initSAT()` (`SATcontrol.ino:3721-3728`). There is no deferred retry, unlike the boot `CS=0` (`SATcontrol.ino:3711-3712`, `SATcontrol.ino:4583-4589`). On a PIC board `pmChainStart()` queues them one at a time: the next `PM=` goes out when the boiler answered the pending ID or after 60 s, and an ID that timed out is asked once more (`OTGW-Core.ino:3895-3982`, TASK-1315). On OT-Direct all three are queued at once (`OTGW-Core.ino:3950-3958`, `OTDirect.ino:3369-3392`) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351): before TASK-1315 only `PM=48` survived the PIC queue. Enabling SAT later sends no `PM=` (`SATcontrol.ino:1950-1958`). | differs (verified) | PIC part fixed by TASK-1315. Remaining: no `PM=` when SAT is enabled after boot. | Send them also when SAT is enabled after boot. |

With the default AUTO (`SATtypes.h:472`), the detected manufacturer is used (`SATcontrol.ino:144-149`). That is known only if MsgID 3 was already seen (`OTGW-Core.ino:3076`). Like the `PM=` commands, it is queued only when the command interface exists at that moment, with no retry (`SATcontrol.ino:3725`). | differs (verified) | Small. The message interval is not shortened for these boilers when they are not yet detected at boot. Neither code says why `MI=500` is needed. | Document only. |

When NTP is not set at boot, the restore is tried once more after NTP becomes available (`SATcontrol.ino:4512-4519`). | differs (verified) | The first ticks after a restart differ. ### The most important differences, in plain words (for Sergeant D)

1. **Overshoot protection is on in the firmware and off in thermo-nova.**
   - In thermo-nova it defaults to off and cannot be switched on from the user interface.
   - With it off, PWM does not touch the setpoint at all: no "10 in the off phase", no "return + 18" hold, no "flow minus 1" suppression. It only sets the modulation limit to 0 (row 49).
   - The firmware does all of that by default.
   - Answered: on by default for all heating systems (entry 20) (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).
2. **Different "no heat needed" threshold and CH switch.**
   - Thermo-nova uses one value, 22 °C, both for "is there demand" and for "switch CH on".
   - The firmware uses 28.2 °C (radiators) or 21 °C (underfloor) for demand and for CH: CH=0 at or below that value or in the PWM OFF phase, otherwise CH=1 (rows 11 and 12; SATchEnable.h:26-52). Answered: entries 7, 9, 11 and 17 (corrected 2026-10-24, TASK-1351).
3. **Hot water.**
   - During a hot-water draw, thermo-nova keeps writing and opens the modulation limit to the maximum. The firmware stops writing, so a modulation limit of 0 from PWM can stay in place (row 28).
   - After a draw, thermo-nova waits 300 s before it reads hot flow water as overshoot. That wait exists in the firmware code but never activates (row 29).
4. **Falling demand in continuous mode.**
   - When demand drops while the boiler water is hot, thermo-nova holds the setpoint at its previous value.
   - The firmware pushes the setpoint up to "flow minus 2". Example: previous 40, request 38, flow 50 gives 40 in thermo-nova and 48 in the firmware (row 42).
5. **Maximum flow temperature.**
   - Thermo-nova defaults to 55 °C (radiators) or 50 °C (underfloor), and the user can change it.
   - The firmware allows up to 62 °C or 45 °C, also respects the boiler's own maximum, and has no user setting (rows 38 and 39).
   - With a boiler that reports its own lower maximum, the firmware's "full demand ends PWM" rule can never fire (row 41).
6. **Missing outside temperature.** Thermo-nova stops writing. The firmware carries on with 0 °C or 10 °C as the outside temperature (row 32).
7. **Immergas.** Thermo-nova sends the modulation limit, capped at 80, in the special `TP=11:12` command. The firmware sends the flow setpoint in that command and caps the normal modulation limit at 80 instead (rows 17 and 18).
8. **Slower loop.** Thermo-nova recalculates and resends every 5 s. The firmware recalculates every 30 s and resends only on change. It does refresh the PWM suppression setpoint every 3 s (rows 6 and 7).
9. **Several defaults differ:**
   - cycles per hour 3 against 4;
   - curve coefficient 1.5 against 2.0;
   - overshoot margin 2.0 against 3.0;
   - presets: comfort 21 against 20, sleep 16 against 15, away 15 against 10;
   - window delay 60 s against 15 s.

   See rows 50 to 54.
10. **Firmware-only safety additions.** These have no thermo-nova counterpart: the `CS=0` release at boot and on disable, the safety trip, the boiler-fault skip, the MQTT-loss fallback and the fixed 45 °C safe setpoint (rows 2, 31, 34 to 36).

### Not found in the code / unclear

1. Whether the PIC or the boiler keeps an `MM` override while the firmware sends nothing (DHW, fault, trip) was not checked in PIC code.
2. Whether `MM` (MsgID 14) also limits the boiler during hot water is not shown in either code (row 28).
3. The effect of a temperature value in Immergas `TP=11:12` on a real boiler is unknown (row 17).
4. Why thermo-nova sends `PM=25` after every `CS`, and `MI=500` for three brands, is not stated in its code (rows 4 and 9).
5. The firmware companion section did not check how old the boiler-side values in `OTcurrentSystemState` may be when SAT uses them. Thermo-nova has no age check either: `MAX_BOILER_TEMPERATURE_AGE` is defined but unused (`const.py:40`).
6. Whether the firmware has an equivalent of thermo-nova's "PWM enabled but IDLE, MM still 0" case belongs to the PWM comparison (row 14).
7. Whether the thermo-nova and firmware manual PID gains use the same units was not compared (row 55).
8. The sources disagree on what `CS=0` does to the CH bit. The firmware comment says the PIC leaves it (`OTDirect.ino:2756-2758`). The OTGW command documentation says `CS=0` clears it (`other-projects/OTGW-documentation/OTGW-Commands.md:492`). `gateway.asm` was not read.
9. The two firmware findings marked "derived from code" (row 3, `PM=` collapse; row 10, first `MM=0`/`CH=0` skipped) were not reproduced on a bench.

---

## Not found in the code / unclear (assembly)

These points were found while assembling. The area sections above are left unchanged, so they still contain the two wordings corrected here.

1. **Area 3, row "Requested setpoint: rooms aggregate", says the firmware uses "third-highest + 5 for 4" zones.** The firmware sorts the zone outputs ascending (SATcontrol.ino:4870-4879) and picks index `ceil(0.75 * N) - 1` (SATcontrol.ino:4881). For 4 zones that is index 2, the second highest. Area 2 row 39 and area 6 row 13 say "second highest", and area 3's own example (30/35/40/45 gives 45 = 40 + 5) also fits the second highest.
2. **Area 1, "Not found" item 5, cites `SATtypes.h:465` for the firmware overshoot-protection default.** Line 465 is `bForcePWM`. The overshoot-protection default `true` is at SATtypes.h:466, as area 4 and area 8 cite.
3. **Area 1, "Not found" item 1, says the thermo-nova pin could not be confirmed.** Since then the repository records it: CLAUDE.md:472 states that the local copy is identical to commit `7beaaf531ee99dc74a19ac74f3c06ce7f2922cfb` (checked 2026-10-22). This assembly did not repeat that check.
4. **Verdict words differ between areas for the same behaviour** (see the note above the totals table). The totals count each area's rows as written; no verdict was changed at assembly.
